```
1→"""Concern generators bridging mechanical findings to holistic review cues."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections import defaultdict
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from desloppify.base.registry import JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
     9→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    10→
    11→from .constants import MIN_FILES_FOR_SMELL_PATTERN, MIN_FILES_FOR_SYSTEMIC
    12→from .signals import _extract_signals, _has_elevated_signals
    13→from .state import _group_by_file, _open_issues
    14→from .text import _build_evidence, _build_question, _build_summary, _classify
    15→from .types import Concern
    16→from .utils import _fingerprint, _is_dismissed
    17→
    18→
    19→def _try_make_concern(
    20→    *,
    21→    concern_type: str,
    22→    file: str,
    23→    fp_keys: tuple[str, ...],
    24→    all_ids: tuple[str, ...],
    25→    dismissals: dict[str, Any],
    26→    summary: str,
    27→    evidence: tuple[str, ...],
    28→    question: str,
    29→    fp_file: str | None = None,
    30→) -> Concern | None:
    31→    """Create a concern unless a matching dismissal exists."""
    32→    fp = _fingerprint(concern_type, fp_file if fp_file is not None else file, fp_keys)
    33→    if _is_dismissed(dismissals, fp, all_ids):
    34→        return None
    35→    return Concern(
    36→        type=concern_type,
    37→        file=file,
    38→        summary=summary,
    39→        evidence=evidence,
    40→        question=question,
    41→        fingerprint=fp,
    42→        source_issues=all_ids,
    43→    )
    44→
    45→
    46→def _file_concerns(state: StateModel, dismissals: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Concern]:
    47→    """Build per-file concerns from aggregated judgment detector signals."""
    48→    by_file = _group_by_file(state)
    49→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
    50→
    51→    for file, all_issues in by_file.items():
    52→        judgment = [
    53→            finding
    54→            for finding in all_issues
    55→            if finding.get("detector", "") in JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
    56→        ]
    57→        if not judgment:
    58→            continue
    59→
    60→        judgment_dets = {finding.get("detector", "") for finding in judgment}
    61→        elevated = _has_elevated_signals(judgment)
    62→
    63→        mechanical_count = len(all_issues)
    64→        if len(judgment_dets) < 2 and not elevated:
    65→            if not (len(judgment_dets) >= 1 and mechanical_count >= 3):
    66→                continue
    67→
    68→        signals = _extract_signals(judgment)
    69→        concern_type = _classify(judgment_dets, signals)
    70→        all_ids = tuple(sorted(finding.get("id", "") for finding in judgment))
    71→        fp_keys = tuple(sorted(judgment_dets))
    72→
    73→        concern = _try_make_concern(
    74→            concern_type=concern_type,
    75→            file=file,
    76→            fp_keys=fp_keys,
    77→            all_ids=all_ids,
    78→            dismissals=dismissals,
    79→            summary=_build_summary(concern_type, judgment_dets, signals),
    80→            evidence=_build_evidence(judgment, signals),
    81→            question=_build_question(judgment_dets, signals),
    82→        )
    83→        if concern is not None:
    84→            concerns.append(concern)
    85→
    86→    return concerns
    87→
    88→
    89→def _cross_file_patterns(state: StateModel, dismissals: dict[str, Any]) -> list[Concern]:
    90→    """Build systemic concerns for detector-combo patterns across files."""
    91→    by_file = _group_by_file(state)
    92→
    93→    profile_to_files: dict[frozenset[str], list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
    94→    for file, issues in by_file.items():
    95→        detectors = frozenset(
    96→            finding.get("detector", "")
    97→            for finding in issues
    98→            if finding.get("detector", "") in JUDGMENT_DETECTORS
    99→        )
   100→        if len(detectors) >= 2:
   101→            profile_to_files[detectors].append(file)
   102→
   103→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   104→    for det_combo, files in profile_to_files.items():
   105→        if len(files) < MIN_FILES_FOR_SYSTEMIC:
   106→            continue
   107→
   108→        sorted_files = sorted(files)
   109→        combo_names = tuple(sorted(det_combo))
   110→        all_ids = tuple(
   111→            sorted(
   112→                finding.get("id", "")
   113→                for file in sorted_files
   114→                for finding in by_file[file]
   115→                if finding.get("detector", "") in det_combo
   116→            )
   117→        )
   118→        concern = _try_make_concern(
   119→            concern_type="systemic_pattern",
   120→            file=sorted_files[0],
   121→            fp_file=",".join(sorted_files[:5]),
   122→            fp_keys=combo_names,
   123→            all_ids=all_ids,
   124→            dismissals=dismissals,
   125→            summary=(
   126→                f"{len(files)} files share the same problem pattern "
   127→                f"({', '.join(combo_names)})"
   128→            ),
   129→            evidence=(
   130→                f"Affected files: {', '.join(sorted_files[:10])}",
   131→                f"Shared detectors: {', '.join(combo_names)}",
   132→                f"Total files: {len(files)}",
   133→            ),
   134→            question=(
   135→                f"These {len(files)} files all have the same combination "
   136→                f"of issues ({', '.join(combo_names)}). Is this a systemic "
   137→                "pattern that should be addressed at the architecture level "
   138→                "(shared base class, framework change, lint rule), or are "
   139→                "these independent issues that happen to look similar?"
   140→            ),
   141→        )
   142→        if concern is not None:
   143→            concerns.append(concern)
   144→
   145→    return concerns
   146→
   147→
   148→def _systemic_smell_patterns(
   149→    state: StateModel,
   150→    dismissals: dict[str, Any],
   151→) -> list[Concern]:
   152→    """Build systemic concerns when a single smell appears across many files."""
   153→    smell_files: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
   154→    smell_ids_map: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
   155→
   156→    for finding in _open_issues(state):
   157→        if finding.get("detector") != "smells":
   158→            continue
   159→        detail = finding.get("detail", {})
   160→        smell_id = detail.get("smell_id", "") if isinstance(detail, dict) else ""
   161→        filepath = finding.get("file", "")
   162→        if smell_id and filepath and filepath != ".":
   163→            smell_files[smell_id].append(filepath)
   164→            smell_ids_map[smell_id].append(finding.get("id", ""))
   165→
   166→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   167→    for smell_id, files in smell_files.items():
   168→        unique_files = sorted(set(files))
   169→        if len(unique_files) < MIN_FILES_FOR_SMELL_PATTERN:
   170→            continue
   171→
   172→        all_ids = tuple(sorted(smell_ids_map[smell_id]))
   173→        concern = _try_make_concern(
   174→            concern_type="systemic_smell",
   175→            file=unique_files[0],
   176→            fp_file=smell_id,
   177→            fp_keys=(smell_id,),
   178→            all_ids=all_ids,
   179→            dismissals=dismissals,
   180→            summary=(
   181→                f"'{smell_id}' appears in {len(unique_files)} files — "
   182→                "likely a systemic pattern"
   183→            ),
   184→            evidence=(
   185→                f"Smell: {smell_id}",
   186→                f"Affected files ({len(unique_files)}): {', '.join(unique_files[:10])}",
   187→            ),
   188→            question=(
   189→                f"The smell '{smell_id}' appears across {len(unique_files)} files. "
   190→                "Is this a codebase-wide convention that should be addressed "
   191→                "systemically (lint rule, shared utility, architecture change), "
   192→                "or are these independent occurrences?"
   193→            ),
   194→        )
   195→        if concern is not None:
   196→            concerns.append(concern)
   197→
   198→    return concerns
   199→
   200→
   201→_GENERATORS = [_file_concerns, _cross_file_patterns, _systemic_smell_patterns]
   202→
   203→
   204→def generate_concerns(state: StateModel) -> list[Concern]:
   205→    """Run all concern generators against current state."""
   206→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})
   207→    concerns: list[Concern] = []
   208→    seen_fingerprints: set[str] = set()
   209→
   210→    for generator in _GENERATORS:
   211→        for concern in generator(state, dismissals):
   212→            if concern.fingerprint not in seen_fingerprints:
   213→                seen_fingerprints.add(concern.fingerprint)
   214→                concerns.append(concern)
   215→
   216→    concerns.sort(key=lambda concern: (concern.type, concern.file))
   217→    return concerns
   218→
   219→
   220→def cleanup_stale_dismissals(state: StateModel) -> int:
   221→    """Remove concern dismissals whose source issues all disappeared."""
   222→    dismissals = state.get("concern_dismissals", {})
   223→    if not dismissals:
   224→        return 0
   225→    open_ids = {finding.get("id", "") for finding in _open_issues(state)}
   226→    stale_fingerprints = [
   227→        fingerprint
   228→        for fingerprint, entry in dismissals.items()
   229→        if entry.get("source_issue_ids")
   230→        and not any(source_id in open_ids for source_id in entry["source_issue_ids"])
   231→    ]
   232→    for fingerprint in stale_fingerprints:
   233→        del dismissals[fingerprint]
   234→    return len(stale_fingerprints)
   235→
   236→
   237→__all__ = [
   238→    "_cross_file_patterns",
   239→    "_file_concerns",
   240→    "cleanup_stale_dismissals",
   241→    "generate_concerns",
   242→]
   243→
```

```
    62→@dataclass
    63→class ReviewPrepareOptions:
    64→    """Configuration bundle for per-file review preparation."""
    65→
    66→    max_files: int | None = None
    67→    max_age_days: int = 30
    68→    force_refresh: bool = True
    69→    dimensions: list[str] | None = None
    70→    config_dimensions: list[str] | None = None
    71→    files: list[str] | None = None
    72→
    73→
    74→@dataclass
    75→class HolisticReviewPrepareOptions:
    76→    """Configuration bundle for holistic review preparation."""
    77→
    78→    dimensions: list[str] | None = None
    79→    files: list[str] | None = None
    80→    include_full_sweep: bool = False
    81→    max_files_per_batch: int | None = None
    82→    include_issue_history: bool = False
    83→    issue_history_max_issues: int = 30
    84→    issue_history_max_batch_items: int = 20
    85→
    86→def _rel_list(s: set[str] | list[str]) -> list[str]:
    87→    """Normalize a set or list of paths to sorted relative paths (max 10)."""
    88→    if isinstance(s, set):
    89→        return sorted(rel(x) for x in s)[:10]
    90→    return [rel(x) for x in list(s)[:10]]
    91→
    92→
    93→def _normalize_max_files(value: Any) -> int | None:
    94→    """Normalize max_files input: None/<=0 means unlimited."""
    95→    if value in (None, ""):
    96→        return None
    97→    parsed = int(value)
    98→    return parsed if parsed > 0 else None
    99→
   100→
   101→def prepare_review(
   102→    path: Path,
   103→    lang: object,
   104→    state: dict,
   105→    options: ReviewPrepareOptions | None = None,
   106→) -> dict[str, object]:
   107→    """Prepare review data for agent consumption. Returns structured dict.
   108→
   109→    If *files* is provided, skip file_finder (avoids redundant filesystem walks
   110→    when the caller already has the file list, e.g. from _setup_lang).
   111→    """
   112→    resolved_options = options or ReviewPrepareOptions()
   113→    resolved_options.max_files = _normalize_max_files(resolved_options.max_files)
   114→    all_files = (
   115→        resolved_options.files
   116→        if resolved_options.files is not None
   117→        else (lang.file_finder(path) if lang.file_finder else [])
   118→    )
   119→
   120→    # Enable file cache for entire prepare operation — context building,
   121→    # file selection, and content extraction all read the same files.
   122→    already_cached = is_file_cache_enabled()
   123→    if not already_cached:
   124→        enable_file_cache()
   125→    try:
   126→        context = build_review_context(path, lang, state, files=all_files)
   127→        selected = select_files_for_review(
   128→            lang,
   129→            path,
   130→            state,
   131→            options=ReviewSelectionOptions(
   132→                max_files=resolved_options.max_files,
   133→                max_age_days=resolved_options.max_age_days,
   134→                force_refresh=resolved_options.force_refresh,
   135→                files=all_files,
   136→            ),
   137→        )
   138→        file_requests = _build_file_requests(selected, lang, state)
   139→    finally:
   140→        if not already_cached:
   141→            disable_file_cache()
   142→
   143→    default_dims, dimension_prompts, system_prompt = load_dimensions_for_lang(lang.name)
   144→    dims = resolve_dimensions(
   145→        cli_dimensions=resolved_options.dimensions,
   146→        config_dimensions=resolved_options.config_dimensions,
   147→        default_dimensions=default_dims,
   148→    )
   149→    lang_guide = get_lang_guidance(lang.name)
   150→    valid_dims = set(dimension_prompts)
   151→    invalid_requested = [
   152→        dim for dim in (resolved_options.dimensions or []) if dim not in valid_dims
   153→    ]
   154→    invalid_config = [
   155→        dim
   156→        for dim in (resolved_options.config_dimensions or [])
   157→        if dim not in valid_dims
   158→    ]
   159→
   160→    return {
   161→        "command": "review",
   162→        "language": lang.name,
   163→        "dimensions": dims,
   164→        "dimension_prompts": {
   165→            d: dimension_prompts[d] for d in dims if d in dimension_prompts
   166→        },
   167→        "lang_guidance": lang_guide,
   168→        "context": serialize_context(context),
   169→        "system_prompt": system_prompt,
   170→        "files": file_requests,
   171→        "total_candidates": len(file_requests),
   172→        "cache_status": {
   173→            "fresh": count_fresh(state, resolved_options.max_age_days),
   174→            "stale": count_stale(state, resolved_options.max_age_days),
   175→            "new": len(file_requests),
   176→        },
   177→        "invalid_dimensions": {
   178→            "requested": invalid_requested,
   179→            "config": invalid_config,
   180→        },
   181→    }
   182→
   183→
   184→def _build_file_requests(files: list[str], lang: object, state: dict) -> list[dict]:
   185→    """Build per-file review request dicts."""
   186→    file_requests = []
   187→    for filepath in files:
   188→        content = read_file_text(abs_path(filepath))
   189→        if content is None:
   190→            continue
   191→
   192→        rpath = rel(filepath)
   193→        zone = "production"
   194→        if lang.zone_map is not None:
   195→            zone = lang.zone_map.get(filepath).value
   196→
   197→        neighbors: dict
   198→        if lang.dep_graph:
   199→            entry = dep_graph_lookup(lang.dep_graph, filepath)
   200→            imports_raw = entry.get("imports", set())
```

```
   254→        dim_ctx.holistic_prompts,

   256→    )
```

```
1→"""Per-detector scoring calculations."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from dataclasses import dataclass, field
     6→
     7→from desloppify.base.scoring_constants import (
     8→    CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS,
     9→    HOLISTIC_MULTIPLIER,
    10→)
    11→from desloppify.engine._scoring.policy.core import (
    12→    FAILURE_STATUSES_BY_MODE,
    13→    SCORING_MODES,
    14→    ScoreMode,
    15→    detector_policy,
    16→)
    17→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import Issue
    18→
    19→# Tiered file-count cap thresholds for non-LOC file-based detectors.
    20→# Controls how many issues per file contribute to the weighted failure sum.
    21→_FILE_CAP_HIGH_THRESHOLD = 6     # issues in file for high cap
    22→_FILE_CAP_MID_THRESHOLD = 3      # issues in file for mid cap
    23→_FILE_CAP_HIGH = 2.0             # cap value at high concentration
    24→_FILE_CAP_MID = 1.5              # cap value at mid concentration
    25→_FILE_CAP_LOW = 1.0              # cap value at low concentration (1-2 issues)
    26→
    27→
    28→def merge_potentials(potentials_by_lang: dict[str, dict[str, int]]) -> dict[str, int]:
    29→    """Sum potentials across languages per detector."""
    30→    merged: dict[str, int] = {}
    31→    for lang_potentials in potentials_by_lang.values():
    32→        for detector, count in lang_potentials.items():
    33→            merged[detector] = merged.get(detector, 0) + count
    34→    return merged
    35→
    36→
    37→def _iter_scoring_candidates(
    38→    detector: str,
    39→    issues: dict[str, Issue],
    40→    excluded_zones: frozenset[str],
    41→):
    42→    """Yield in-scope issues for a detector (zone-filtered)."""
    43→    for issue in issues.values():
    44→        if issue.get("suppressed"):
    45→            continue
    46→        if issue.get("detector") != detector:
    47→            continue
    48→        if issue.get("zone", "production") in excluded_zones:
    49→            continue
    50→        yield issue
    51→
    52→
    53→def _issue_weight(issue: Issue, *, use_loc_weight: bool) -> float:
    54→    """Compute the scoring weight for a single issue."""
    55→    if use_loc_weight:
    56→        return issue.get("detail", {}).get("loc_weight", 1.0)
    57→    return CONFIDENCE_WEIGHTS.get(issue.get("confidence", "medium"), 0.7)
    58→
    59→
    60→def _file_count_cap(issues_in_file: int) -> float:
    61→    """Tiered cap for non-LOC file-based detectors.
    62→
    63→    Keeps file-count denominator semantics while preserving concentration signal:
    64→    1-2 issues => _FILE_CAP_LOW, 3-5 => _FILE_CAP_MID, 6+ => _FILE_CAP_HIGH.
    65→    """
    66→    if issues_in_file >= _FILE_CAP_HIGH_THRESHOLD:
    67→        return _FILE_CAP_HIGH
    68→    if issues_in_file >= _FILE_CAP_MID_THRESHOLD:
    69→        return _FILE_CAP_MID
    70→    return _FILE_CAP_LOW
    71→
    72→
    73→@dataclass
    74→class _ModeAccum:
    75→    """Per-mode accumulator for file-based detector scoring."""
    76→
    77→    by_file: dict[str, float] = field(default_factory=dict)
    78→    by_file_count: dict[str, int] = field(default_factory=dict)
    79→    file_cap: dict[str, float] = field(default_factory=dict)
    80→    holistic_sum: float = 0.0
    81→    issue_count: int = 0
    82→
    83→
    84→def _file_based_failures_by_mode(
    85→    detector: str,
    86→    issues: dict[str, Issue],
    87→    policy,
    88→) -> dict[ScoreMode, tuple[int, float]]:
    89→    """Accumulate weighted failures by score mode for file-based detectors."""
    90→    accum: dict[ScoreMode, _ModeAccum] = {mode: _ModeAccum() for mode in SCORING_MODES}
    91→
    92→    for issue in _iter_scoring_candidates(detector, issues, policy.excluded_zones):
    93→        status = issue.get("status", "open")
    94→        holistic = issue.get("file") == "." and issue.get("detail", {}).get(
    95→            "holistic"
    96→        )
    97→
    98→        for mode in SCORING_MODES:
    99→            if status not in FAILURE_STATUSES_BY_MODE[mode]:
   100→                continue
   101→
   102→            if holistic:
   103→                accum[mode].holistic_sum += (
   104→                    _issue_weight(issue, use_loc_weight=False) * HOLISTIC_MULTIPLIER
   105→                )
   106→                accum[mode].issue_count += 1
   107→                continue
   108→
   109→            weight = _issue_weight(issue, use_loc_weight=policy.use_loc_weight)
   110→            file_key = issue.get("file", "")
   111→            a = accum[mode]
   112→            a.by_file[file_key] = a.by_file.get(file_key, 0.0) + weight
   113→            a.by_file_count[file_key] = a.by_file_count.get(file_key, 0) + 1
   114→            if policy.use_loc_weight and file_key not in a.file_cap:
   115→                a.file_cap[file_key] = weight
   116→            a.issue_count += 1
   117→
   118→    out: dict[ScoreMode, tuple[int, float]] = {}
   119→    for mode in SCORING_MODES:
   120→        a = accum[mode]
   121→        if policy.use_loc_weight:
   122→            weighted = sum(
   123→                min(weighted_sum, a.file_cap.get(file_key, weighted_sum))
   124→                for file_key, weighted_sum in a.by_file.items()
   125→            )
   126→        else:
   127→            weighted = sum(
   128→                min(weighted_sum, _file_count_cap(a.by_file_count.get(file_key, 0)))
   129→                for file_key, weighted_sum in a.by_file.items()
   130→            )
   131→        out[mode] = (a.issue_count, weighted + a.holistic_sum)
   132→    return out
   133→
   134→
   135→def detector_stats_by_mode(
   136→    detector: str,
   137→    issues: dict[str, Issue],
   138→    potential: int,
   139→) -> dict[ScoreMode, tuple[float, int, float]]:
   140→    """Compute (pass_rate, issue_count, weighted_failures) for each score mode."""
   141→    if potential <= 0:
   142→        return {mode: (1.0, 0, 0.0) for mode in SCORING_MODES}
   143→
   144→    # Review and concern issues are scored via subjective assessments only —
   145→    # exclude them from the detection-side scoring pipeline so resolving these
   146→    # issues never changes the score directly.
   147→    if detector in ("review", "concerns"):
   148→        return {mode: (1.0, 0, 0.0) for mode in SCORING_MODES}
   149→
   150→    policy = detector_policy(detector)
   151→
   152→    if policy.file_based:
   153→        mode_failures = _file_based_failures_by_mode(detector, issues, policy)
   154→    else:
   155→        issue_count: dict[ScoreMode, int] = {mode: 0 for mode in SCORING_MODES}
   156→        weighted_failures: dict[ScoreMode, float] = {
   157→            mode: 0.0 for mode in SCORING_MODES
   158→        }
   159→
   160→        for issue in _iter_scoring_candidates(
   161→            detector, issues, policy.excluded_zones
   162→        ):
   163→            status = issue.get("status", "open")
   164→            weight = _issue_weight(issue, use_loc_weight=False)
   165→            for mode in SCORING_MODES:
   166→                if status not in FAILURE_STATUSES_BY_MODE[mode]:
   167→                    continue
   168→                issue_count[mode] += 1
   169→                weighted_failures[mode] += weight
   170→
   171→        mode_failures = {
   172→            mode: (issue_count[mode], weighted_failures[mode]) for mode in SCORING_MODES
   173→        }
   174→
   175→    out: dict[ScoreMode, tuple[float, int, float]] = {}
   176→    for mode in SCORING_MODES:
   177→        issues, weighted = mode_failures[mode]
   178→        pass_rate = max(0.0, (potential - weighted) / potential)
   179→        out[mode] = (pass_rate, issues, weighted)
   180→    return out
   181→
   182→
   183→def detector_pass_rate(
   184→    detector: str,
   185→    issues: dict[str, Issue],
   186→    potential: int,
   187→    *,
   188→    strict: bool = False,
   189→) -> tuple[float, int, float]:
   190→    """Pass rate for one detector.
   191→
   192→    Returns (pass_rate, issue_count, weighted_failures).
   193→    Zero potential -> (1.0, 0, 0.0).
   194→    """
   195→    mode: ScoreMode = "strict" if strict else "lenient"
   196→    return detector_stats_by_mode(detector, issues, potential)[mode]
   197→
   198→
   199→__all__ = [
   200→    "detector_pass_rate",
   201→    "detector_stats_by_mode",
   202→    "merge_potentials",
   203→]
   204→
```

```
1→"""Packet sanitization, hashing, and artifact layout helpers for review batches."""
     2→

     4→
     5→import json

     7→from datetime import UTC, datetime

     9→from pathlib import Path

    12→from desloppify.base.exception_sets import PacketValidationError
    13→
    14→_BLIND_PACKET_DROP_KEYS = {
    15→    "narrative",
    16→    "next_command",
    17→    "score_snapshot",
    18→    "strict_target",
    19→    "strict_target_progress",
    20→    "subjective_at_target",

    33→
    34→def write_packet_snapshot(
    35→    packet: dict[str, Any],
    36→    *,
    37→    stamp: str,
    38→    review_packet_dir: Path,
    39→    blind_path: Path,
    40→    safe_write_text_fn,
    41→) -> tuple[Path, Path]:
    42→    """Persist immutable and blind packet snapshots for runner workflows."""
    43→    review_packet_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
    44→    packet_path = review_packet_dir / f"holistic_packet_{stamp}.json"
    45→    safe_write_text_fn(packet_path, json.dumps(packet, indent=2) + "\n")
    46→    blind_packet = _build_blind_packet(packet)
    47→    safe_write_text_fn(blind_path, json.dumps(blind_packet, indent=2) + "\n")
    48→    return packet_path, blind_path
    49→
    50→
    51→def _build_blind_packet(packet: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    52→    """Return a blind-review packet with score anchoring metadata removed."""
    53→    blind = deepcopy(packet)
    54→    for key in _BLIND_PACKET_DROP_KEYS:
    55→        blind.pop(key, None)
    56→
    57→    config = blind.get("config")
    58→    if isinstance(config, dict):
    59→        sanitized = _sanitize_blind_config(config)
    60→        if sanitized:
    61→            blind["config"] = sanitized
    62→        else:
    63→            blind.pop("config", None)
    64→    return blind
    65→
    66→
    67→def build_blind_packet(packet: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    68→    """Public wrapper for blind packet sanitization."""
    69→    return _build_blind_packet(packet)
    70→
    71→
    72→def run_stamp() -> str:
    73→    """Stable UTC run stamp for artifact paths."""
    74→    return datetime.now(UTC).strftime("%Y%m%d_%H%M%S")
    75→
    76→
    77→def _sanitize_blind_config(config: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    78→    """Drop score/target hints from config while preserving unrelated options."""
    79→    sanitized: dict[str, Any] = {}
    80→    for key, value in config.items():
    81→        lowered = key.strip().lower()
    82→        if not lowered:
    83→            continue
    84→        if lowered in _BLIND_CONFIG_SCORE_HINT_KEYS:
    85→            continue
    86→        if "target" in lowered:
    87→            continue
    88→        if lowered.endswith("_score"):
    89→            continue
    90→        sanitized[key] = value
    91→    return sanitized
    92→
    93→
    94→def sha256_file(path: Path) -> str | None:
    95→    """Compute sha256 hex digest for path contents (or None on read failure)."""
    96→    try:
    97→        data = path.read_bytes()
    98→    except OSError:
    99→        return None
   100→    return sha256(data).hexdigest()
   101→
   102→
   103→def build_batch_import_provenance(
   104→    *,
   105→    runner: str,
   106→    blind_packet_path: Path,
   107→    run_stamp: str,

   112→    batch_indexes_1 = sorted({int(index) + 1 for index in batch_indexes})
   113→    return {

   115→        "blind": True,
   116→        "runner": runner,
   117→        "run_stamp": run_stamp,
   118→        "created_at": datetime.now(UTC).isoformat(timespec="seconds"),
   119→        "batch_count": len(batch_indexes_1),
   120→        "batch_indexes": batch_indexes_1,
   121→        "packet_path": str(blind_packet_path),
   122→        "packet_sha256": packet_hash,
   123→    }
   124→
   125→
   126→def selected_batch_indexes(
   127→    *,
   128→    raw_selection: str | None,
   129→    batch_count: int,
   130→    parse_fn,
   131→    colorize_fn,
   132→) -> list[int]:
   133→    """Validate selected batch indexes or raise CommandError."""
   134→    try:
   135→        selected = parse_fn(raw_selection, batch_count)
   136→    except ValueError as exc:
   137→        raise PacketValidationError(str(exc), exit_code=2) from exc
   138→    if selected:
   139→        return selected
   140→    raise PacketValidationError("no batches selected", exit_code=2)
   141→
   142→
   143→def prepare_run_artifacts(
   144→    *,
   145→    stamp: str,
   146→    selected_indexes: list[int],
   147→    batches: list[dict[str, Any]],
   148→    packet_path: Path,

   154→) -> tuple[Path, Path, dict[int, Path], dict[int, Path], dict[int, Path]]:
   155→    """Build prompt/output/log paths and persist prompts for selected batches."""
   156→    run_dir = run_root / stamp
   157→    prompts_dir = run_dir / "prompts"
   158→    results_dir = run_dir / "results"
   159→    logs_dir = run_dir / "logs"
   160→    prompts_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   161→    results_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   162→    logs_dir.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
   163→
   164→    selected_1_based = [idx + 1 for idx in selected_indexes]
   165→    print(colorize_fn(f"\n  Running holistic batches: {selected_1_based}", "bold"))
   166→    print(colorize_fn(f"  Run artifacts: {run_dir}", "dim"))
   167→
   168→    prompt_files: dict[int, Path] = {}
   169→    output_files: dict[int, Path] = {}
   170→    log_files: dict[int, Path] = {}
   171→    for idx in selected_indexes:
   172→        batch = batches[idx] if isinstance(batches[idx], dict) else {}
   173→        prompt_text = build_prompt_fn(
   174→            repo_root=repo_root,
   175→            packet_path=packet_path,
   176→            batch_index=idx,
   177→            batch=batch,
   178→        )
   179→        prompt_file = prompts_dir / f"batch-{idx + 1}.md"
   180→        output_file = results_dir / f"batch-{idx + 1}.raw.txt"
   181→        log_file = logs_dir / f"batch-{idx + 1}.log"
   182→        safe_write_text_fn(prompt_file, prompt_text)
   183→        prompt_files[idx] = prompt_file
   184→        output_files[idx] = output_file
   185→        log_files[idx] = log_file
   186→    return run_dir, logs_dir, prompt_files, output_files, log_files
   187→
```

```
    64→class TestSuppressionAccounting:
    65→    def test_merge_scan_records_ignored_metrics_in_history_and_diff(self):
    66→        st = empty_state()
    67→        issues = [
    68→            _make_raw_issue("smells::a.py::x", detector="smells", file="a.py"),
    69→            _make_raw_issue("smells::b.py::y", detector="smells", file="b.py"),
    70→            _make_raw_issue("logs::c.py::z", detector="logs", file="c.py"),
    71→        ]
    72→
    73→        diff = merge_scan(
    74→            st, issues, MergeScanOptions(lang="python", ignore=["smells::*"], force_resolve=True)
    75→        )
    76→
    77→        assert diff["ignored"] == 2
    78→        assert diff["raw_issues"] == 3
    79→        assert diff["suppressed_pct"] == pytest.approx(66.7, abs=0.1)
    80→
    81→        hist = st["scan_history"][-1]
    82→        assert hist["ignored"] == 2
    83→        assert hist["raw_issues"] == 3
    84→        assert hist["suppressed_pct"] == pytest.approx(66.7, abs=0.1)
    85→        assert hist["ignore_patterns"] == 1

   116→class TestScoreAntiGaming:
   117→    def test_scan_history_records_subjective_integrity_snapshot(self):
   118→        st = empty_state()
   119→        st["subjective_assessments"] = {
   120→            "naming_quality": {"score": 95},
   121→            "logic_clarity": {"score": 95},
   122→        }
   123→        merge_scan(
   124→            st,

   126→            MergeScanOptions(lang="python", potentials={"unused": 0}, force_resolve=True, subjective_integrity_target=95.0),
   127→        )
   128→
   129→        hist = st["scan_history"][-1]
   130→        assert hist["subjective_integrity"]["status"] == "penalized"
   131→        assert hist["subjective_integrity"]["matched_count"] == 2
   132→        assert hist["subjective_integrity"]["reset_count"] == 2

   134→    def test_save_state_preserves_subjective_integrity_target(self, tmp_path):
   135→        st = empty_state()
   136→        st["subjective_assessments"] = {
   137→            "naming_quality": {"score": 95},
   138→            "logic_clarity": {"score": 95},
   139→        }
   140→        merge_scan(
   141→            st,
   142→            [],
   143→            MergeScanOptions(lang="python", potentials={"unused": 0}, force_resolve=True, subjective_integrity_target=95.0),
   144→        )
   145→
   146→        save_path = tmp_path / "state.json"
   147→        save_state(st, save_path)
   148→        reloaded = load_state(save_path)
   149→
   150→        assert reloaded["subjective_integrity"]["status"] == "penalized"
   151→        assert reloaded["subjective_integrity"]["target_score"] == 95.0
   152→        assert reloaded["dimension_scores"]["Naming quality"]["score"] == 0.0
   153→        assert reloaded["dimension_scores"]["Logic clarity"]["score"] == 0.0

   155→    def test_manual_fixed_does_not_improve_verified_until_scan_confirms(self):
   156→        from desloppify.state import resolve_issues
   157→
   158→        st = empty_state()
   159→        issue = _make_raw_issue("unused::a.py::x", detector="unused", file="a.py")
   160→        merge_scan(
   161→            st,
   162→            [issue],
   163→            MergeScanOptions(lang="python", potentials={"unused": 1}, force_resolve=True),
   164→        )
   165→        before_strict = st["strict_score"]
   166→        before_verified = st["verified_strict_score"]
   167→
   168→        resolve_issues(
   169→            st,
   170→            "unused::a.py::x",
   171→            "fixed",
   172→            note="removed symbol",
   173→            attestation="I have actually fixed this and I am not gaming the score.",
   174→        )
   175→        # strict_score should improve (fixed is not a failure in strict mode)
   176→        assert st["strict_score"] > before_strict
   177→        # verified_strict_score should NOT improve (fixed still counts as failing)
   178→        assert st["verified_strict_score"] == before_verified
   179→
   180→        # Scan confirms absence — issue gets scan-verified metadata but keeps
   181→        # its "fixed" status.  verified_strict still treats "fixed" as failing,
   182→        # so the score stays unchanged; however the attestation records that the
   183→        # scan corroborated the manual resolution.
   184→        merge_scan(
   185→            st,
   186→            [],
   187→            MergeScanOptions(lang="python", potentials={"unused": 1}, force_resolve=True),
   188→        )
   189→        assert st["verified_strict_score"] == before_verified
   190→        attestation = st["issues"]["unused::a.py::x"].get("resolution_attestation", {})
   191→        assert attestation.get("scan_verified") is True
   192→
   193→    def test_ignore_pattern_suppresses_and_excludes_from_scoring(self):
   194→        from desloppify.state import remove_ignored_issues
   195→
   196→        st = empty_state()
   197→        issue = _make_raw_issue("unused::a.py::x", detector="unused", file="a.py")
   198→        merge_scan(
   199→            st,
   200→            [issue],
   201→            MergeScanOptions(lang="python", potentials={"unused": 1}, force_resolve=True),
   202→        )
   203→        strict_before = st["strict_score"]
   204→
   205→        removed = remove_ignored_issues(st, "unused::*")
   206→        assert removed == 1
   207→        assert st["issues"]["unused::a.py::x"]["suppressed"] is True
   208→        # Suppressed issues are invisible to scoring — score should improve
   209→        assert st["strict_score"] >= strict_before
   210→
```

```
183→def _build_claude_launch_prompt(

   192→    """Build a copy/paste-ready prompt for a Claude blind reviewer subagent."""
   193→    header = (
   194→        "# Claude Blind Reviewer Launch Prompt\n\n"
   195→        "You are an isolated blind reviewer. Do not use prior chat context, "
   196→        "prior score history, or target-score anchoring.\n\n"

   202→    )

   204→    raw_batches = packet.get("investigation_batches", [])
   205→    if not isinstance(raw_batches, list):
   206→        raw_batches = []

   208→    dim_prompts: dict[str, dict[str, object]] = (
   209→        raw_dim_prompts if isinstance(raw_dim_prompts, dict) else {}
   210→    )
   211→    batches = explode_to_single_dimension(
   212→        [b for b in raw_batches if isinstance(b, dict)],
   213→        dimension_prompts=dim_prompts or None,
   214→    )
   215→

   221→        all_dims.update(ctx.dimension_set)

   223→

   229→        section += render_seed_files_block(ctx)
   230→        section += render_historical_focus(batch)
   231→        section += render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch)
   232→        batch_sections.append(section)
   233→
   234→    if not combined_cap:
   235→        combined_cap = 10
   236→
   237→    output_schema = (
```

```
1→"""Small utility helpers for concern generation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import hashlib
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from .types import ConcernSignals, SignalKey
     9→
    10→
    11→def _update_max_signal(signals: ConcernSignals, key: SignalKey, value: object) -> None:
    12→    """Update numeric signal key with max(existing, value) when value is valid."""
    13→    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, int | float) or value <= 0:
    14→        return
    15→    current = float(signals.get(key, 0.0))
    16→    signals[key] = max(current, float(value))
    17→
    18→
    19→def _fingerprint(concern_type: str, file: str, key_signals: tuple[str, ...]) -> str:
    20→    """Stable hash of (type, file, sorted key signals)."""
    21→    raw = f"{concern_type}::{file}::{','.join(sorted(key_signals))}"
    22→    return hashlib.sha256(raw.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
    23→
    24→
    25→def _is_dismissed(
    26→    dismissals: dict[str, Any], fingerprint: str, source_issue_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    27→) -> bool:
    28→    """Check if a concern was previously dismissed and source issues unchanged."""
    29→    entry = dismissals.get(fingerprint)
    30→    if not isinstance(entry, dict):
    31→        return False
    32→    prev_sources = set(entry.get("source_issue_ids", []))
    33→    return prev_sources == set(source_issue_ids)
    34→
    35→
    36→__all__ = ["_fingerprint", "_is_dismissed", "_update_max_signal"]
    37→
```

```
1→"""Aggregate mechanical detector issues into structured evidence clusters.
     2→
     3→Reads all state issues and produces signal clusters organized for holistic
     4→review context enrichment.
     5→"""
     6→
     7→from __future__ import annotations
     8→
     9→from collections import Counter, defaultdict
    10→from typing import Any
    11→
    12→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
    13→
    14→from ._clusters_complexity import _build_complexity_hotspots
    15→from ._clusters_consistency import _build_duplicate_clusters, _build_naming_drift
    16→from ._clusters_dependency import (
    17→    _build_boundary_violations,
    18→    _build_dead_code,
    19→    _build_deferred_import_density,
    20→    _build_private_crossings,
    21→)
    22→from ._clusters_error_state import _build_error_hotspots, _build_mutable_globals
    23→from ._clusters_organization import (
    24→    _build_flat_dir_issues,
    25→    _build_large_file_distribution,
    26→)
    27→from ._clusters_security import (
    28→    _build_security_hotspots,
    29→    _build_signal_density,
    30→    _build_systemic_patterns,
    31→)
    32→
    33→
    34→def _normalize_allowed_files(
    35→    allowed_files: set[str] | list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None,
    36→) -> set[str] | None:
    37→    """Normalize optional allowed-file scope to slash-normalized relative paths."""
    38→    if allowed_files is None:
    39→        return None
    40→    out: set[str] = set()
    41→    for raw in allowed_files:
    42→        if not isinstance(raw, str):
    43→            continue
    44→        file_path = raw.strip().replace("\\", "/")
    45→        if file_path:
    46→            out.add(file_path)
    47→    return out
    48→
    49→
    50→def gather_mechanical_evidence(
    51→    state: StateModel,
    52→    *,
    53→    allowed_files: set[str] | list[str] | tuple[str, ...] | None = None,
    54→) -> dict[str, Any]:
    55→    """Aggregate open issues into evidence clusters for holistic review."""
    56→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
    57→    if not issues:
    58→        return {}
    59→    allowed_scope = _normalize_allowed_files(allowed_files)
    60→
    61→    by_detector: dict[str, list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
    62→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict]] = defaultdict(list)
    63→    smell_counter: Counter[str] = Counter()
    64→    smell_files: dict[str, list[str]] = defaultdict(list)
    65→
    66→    for issue in issues.values():
    67→        if not isinstance(issue, dict):
    68→            continue
    69→        if issue.get("status") != "open":
    70→            continue
    71→        filepath = issue.get("file", "")
    72→        normalized_file = filepath.strip().replace("\\", "/") if isinstance(filepath, str) else ""
    73→        if allowed_scope is not None and normalized_file not in allowed_scope:
    74→            continue
    75→        det = issue.get("detector", "")
    76→        if det:
    77→            by_detector[det].append(issue)
    78→        if normalized_file and normalized_file != ".":
    79→            by_file[normalized_file].append(issue)
    80→        if det == "smells":
    81→            detail = issue.get("detail", {})
    82→            smell_id = detail.get("smell_id", "") if isinstance(detail, dict) else ""
    83→            if smell_id:
    84→                smell_counter[smell_id] += 1
    85→                smell_files[smell_id].append(normalized_file)
    86→
    87→    if not by_detector:
    88→        return {}
    89→
    90→    evidence: dict[str, Any] = {}
    91→
    92→    complexity_hotspots = _build_complexity_hotspots(by_detector, by_file)
    93→    if complexity_hotspots:
    94→        evidence["complexity_hotspots"] = complexity_hotspots
    95→
    96→    error_hotspots = _build_error_hotspots(by_detector)
    97→    if error_hotspots:
    98→        evidence["error_hotspots"] = error_hotspots
    99→
   100→    mutable_globals = _build_mutable_globals(by_detector)
   101→    if mutable_globals:
   102→        evidence["mutable_globals"] = mutable_globals
   103→
   104→    boundary_violations = _build_boundary_violations(by_detector)
   105→    if boundary_violations:
   106→        evidence["boundary_violations"] = boundary_violations
   107→
   108→    dead_code = _build_dead_code(by_detector)
   109→    if dead_code:
   110→        evidence["dead_code"] = dead_code
   111→
   112→    private_crossings = _build_private_crossings(by_detector)
   113→    if private_crossings:
   114→        evidence["private_crossings"] = private_crossings
   115→
   116→    deferred = _build_deferred_import_density(by_file)
   117→    if deferred:
   118→        evidence["deferred_import_density"] = deferred
   119→
   120→    duplicate_clusters = _build_duplicate_clusters(by_detector)
   121→    if duplicate_clusters:
   122→        evidence["duplicate_clusters"] = duplicate_clusters
   123→
   124→    naming_drift = _build_naming_drift(by_detector)
   125→    if naming_drift:
   126→        evidence["naming_drift"] = naming_drift
   127→
   128→    flat_dir_issues = _build_flat_dir_issues(by_detector)
   129→    if flat_dir_issues:
   130→        evidence["flat_dir_issues"] = flat_dir_issues
   131→
   132→    large_dist = _build_large_file_distribution(by_detector)
   133→    if large_dist:
   134→        evidence["large_file_distribution"] = large_dist
   135→
   136→    security_hotspots = _build_security_hotspots(by_detector)
   137→    if security_hotspots:
   138→        evidence["security_hotspots"] = security_hotspots
   139→
   140→    signal_density = _build_signal_density(by_file)
   141→    if signal_density:
   142→        evidence["signal_density"] = signal_density
   143→
   144→    systemic = _build_systemic_patterns(smell_counter, smell_files)
   145→    if systemic:
   146→        evidence["systemic_patterns"] = systemic
   147→
   148→    pkg_census = _build_package_size_census(by_file)
   149→    if pkg_census:
   150→        evidence["package_size_census"] = pkg_census
   151→
   152→    return evidence
   153→
   154→
   155→def _build_package_size_census(
   156→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict]],
   157→) -> list[dict]:
   158→    """Compute LOC per top-level package.  Flag packages >15% of codebase."""
   159→    pkg_loc: dict[str, int] = {}
   160→    for filepath, issues in by_file.items():
   161→        parts = filepath.replace("\\", "/").split("/")
   162→        pkg = parts[0] if parts else filepath
   163→        # Sum file LOC from structural detail or count issues as proxy.
   164→        file_loc = 0
   165→        for issue in issues:
   166→            if issue.get("detector") == "structural":
   167→                detail = issue.get("detail", {})
   168→                if isinstance(detail, dict):
   169→                    loc_val = detail.get("loc", 0)
   170→                    if isinstance(loc_val, int | float) and loc_val > file_loc:
   171→                        file_loc = int(loc_val)
   172→        if file_loc == 0:
   173→            file_loc = 1  # Count the file at minimum
   174→        pkg_loc[pkg] = pkg_loc.get(pkg, 0) + file_loc
   175→
   176→    total_loc = sum(pkg_loc.values())
   177→    if total_loc == 0:
   178→        return []
   179→
   180→    results = []
   181→    for pkg, loc in sorted(pkg_loc.items(), key=lambda kv: -kv[1]):
   182→        pct = round(100 * loc / total_loc, 1)
   183→        results.append({
   184→            "package": pkg,
   185→            "loc": loc,
   186→            "pct_of_total": pct,
   187→            "disproportionate": pct > 15,
   188→        })
   189→    return results
   190→
   191→
   192→__all__ = ["gather_mechanical_evidence"]
   193→
```

```
    35→def _queue_order(plan: PlanModel) -> list[str]:
    36→    """Return normalized queue order list, seeding default if missing."""
    37→    order = plan.get("queue_order")
    38→    if isinstance(order, list):
    39→        return order
    40→    normalized: list[str] = []

    45→def _skipped_map(plan: PlanModel) -> dict[str, Any]:
    46→    """Return normalized skipped map, seeding default if missing."""
    47→    skipped = plan.get("skipped")
    48→    if isinstance(skipped, dict):
    49→        return cast(dict[str, Any], skipped)
    50→    normalized: dict[str, Any] = {}
    51→    plan["skipped"] = normalized
    52→    return normalized
    53→
    54→
    55→def _cluster_map(plan: PlanModel) -> dict[str, Cluster]:
    56→    """Return normalized cluster map, seeding default if missing."""
    57→    clusters = plan.get("clusters")
    58→    if isinstance(clusters, dict):
    59→        return cast(dict[str, Cluster], clusters)
    60→    normalized: dict[str, Cluster] = {}
    61→    plan["clusters"] = normalized
    62→    return normalized
    63→
    64→
    65→def _triage_meta(plan: PlanModel) -> dict[str, Any]:
    66→    """Return normalized triage metadata map, seeding default if missing."""
    67→    meta = plan.get("epic_triage_meta")
    68→    if isinstance(meta, dict):
    69→        return cast(dict[str, Any], meta)
    70→    normalized: dict[str, Any] = {}
    71→    plan["epic_triage_meta"] = normalized
    72→    return normalized
    73→
    74→
    75→def _execution_log(plan: PlanModel) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:

    77→    log = plan.get("execution_log")
    78→    if isinstance(log, list):
    79→        return [entry for entry in log if isinstance(entry, dict)]
    80→    normalized: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
```

```
    70→

    74→JUDGMENT_DETECTORS: Set[str] = _JudgmentDetectorsView()

    77→def _rebuild_judgment_detectors() -> None:
    78→    _REGISTRY.judgment_detectors.clear()
```

```
1→"""Queue-policy helpers shared by planning render/select modules."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations

     5→from dataclasses import replace

     7→from desloppify.base.config import DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE
     8→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
     9→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.core import (
    10→    QueueBuildOptions,
    11→    QueueVisibility,
    12→    WorkQueueResult,
    13→    _build_work_queue_with_visibility,
    14→    build_work_queue,

    17→
    18→def _subjective_threshold(state: StateModel, *, default: float = DEFAULT_TARGET_STRICT_SCORE) -> float:
    19→    config = state.get("config", {})
    20→    raw_target = default
    21→    if isinstance(config, dict):
    22→        raw_target = config.get("target_strict_score", default)
    23→    try:
    24→        value = float(raw_target)
    25→    except (TypeError, ValueError):
    26→        value = default
    27→    return max(0.0, min(100.0, value))
    28→
    29→
    30→def _queue_shaping_plan_present(plan: dict | None) -> bool:
    31→    if not isinstance(plan, dict):
    32→        return False
    33→    return bool(plan.get("queue_order") or plan.get("overrides") or plan.get("clusters"))
    34→
    35→
    36→def _queue_plan_from_options(options: QueueBuildOptions) -> dict | None:
    37→    if options.context is not None:
    38→        return options.context.plan
    39→    return options.plan
    40→
    41→
    42→def build_open_plan_queue(
    43→    state: StateModel,
    44→    *,
    45→    options: QueueBuildOptions | None = None,
    46→) -> WorkQueueResult:
    47→    """Build one open-status queue with consistent planning policy defaults."""
    48→    opts = replace(options) if options is not None else QueueBuildOptions(count=None)
    49→    if options is None:
    50→        opts = replace(opts, status="open", include_subjective=True)
    51→    elif opts.status == QueueBuildOptions().status:
    52→        opts = replace(opts, status="open")
    53→    if opts.subjective_threshold == QueueBuildOptions().subjective_threshold:
    54→        opts = replace(opts, subjective_threshold=_subjective_threshold(state))
    55→    return build_work_queue(
    56→        state,
    57→        options=opts,
    58→    )
    59→
    60→
    61→def build_execution_queue(
    62→    state: StateModel,
    63→    *,
    64→    options: QueueBuildOptions | None = None,
    65→) -> WorkQueueResult:
    66→    """Build the execution queue that follows the living plan when present."""
    67→    options = options or QueueBuildOptions()
    68→    if not _queue_shaping_plan_present(_queue_plan_from_options(options)):
    69→        return build_work_queue(state, options=options)
    70→    return _build_work_queue_with_visibility(

    81→) -> WorkQueueResult:
    82→    """Build the broader backlog, excluding work already tracked in the plan."""
    83→    options = options or QueueBuildOptions()
    84→    if not _queue_shaping_plan_present(_queue_plan_from_options(options)):
```

Perfect! Now I have all the information I need. Let me compile my findings into a comprehensive report.

Based on my thorough end-to-end tracing of the holistic review process, here's what I found:

## 1. Review Prompt Construction

The review prompt gets built through a **multi-stage serialization pipeline**:

**Stage 1: Holistic Context Building** (`desloppify/intelligence/review/context_holistic/orchestrator.py`)
- `build_holistic_context_model()` orchestrates gathering of codebase-wide context
- Calls specialized functions to build 14 context sections: `architecture`, `coupling`, `conventions`, `errors`, `abstractions`, `dependencies`, `testing`, `api_surface`, `structure`, `codebase_stats`, `authorization`, `ai_debt_signals`, `migration_signals`, `scan_evidence`
- Invokes `gather_mechanical_evidence()` which aggregates all detector issues and clusters them into signal groups

**Stage 2: Evidence Clustering** (`desloppify/intelligence/review/context_holistic/mechanical.py`)
- Mechanical evidence is organized into 13+ evidence clusters based on detector type:
  - `complexity_hotspots` (from structural/smells/responsibility_cohesion detectors)
  - `error_hotspots`, `mutable_globals` (from error detectors)
  - `boundary_violations` (from dependency detectors)
  - `duplicate_clusters`, `naming_drift` (from consistency detectors)
  - `flat_dir_issues`, `large_file_distribution` (from organization detectors)
  - `security_hotspots`, `signal_density`, `systemic_patterns` (from security/signal detectors)
  - `package_size_census` (computed from LOC distributions)
- These are merged into the holistic context's `scan_evidence` section

**Stage 3: Payload Assembly** (`desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare_holistic_orchestration.py`)
- `prepare_holistic_review_payload()` builds the final packet containing:
  - `holistic_context`: dict with all 14 sections (including `scan_evidence` from step 2)
  - `investigation_batches`: list of dimension-scoped investigation batches
  - `dimension_prompts`: the rubrics for each dimension
  - `system_prompt`: calibration and scoring rules
  - `lang_guidance`: language-specific conventions guide

**Stage 4: Blind Packet Creation** (`desloppify/app/commands/review/runner_packets.py`)
- `write_packet_snapshot()` creates two JSON files:
  - Immutable full packet (for auditing)
  - Blind packet (score/target hints removed via `_build_blind_packet()`)
- Blind packet drops: `narrative`, `next_command`, `score_snapshot`, strict targets

**Stage 5: Prompt Rendering** (`desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/prompt_template.py` and `desloppify/app/commands/review/external.py`)
- For **local batch execution**: `render_batch_prompt()` builds a text prompt that includes:
  - Metadata block (repo root, packet path, batch name/rationale)
  - Dimension rubrics with "look_for"/"skip" guidance
  - Rendering of the dimension-focused scan evidence guidance
  - Seed files block
  - Historical issue focus (previous issues for navigation)
  - Mechanical concern signals (8 capped)
  - Task requirements with dimension-specific focus instructions
  - Output JSON schema with all required fields
  
- For **external/Claude sessions** (`_build_claude_launch_prompt()`): Similar structure but copy/paste-ready markdown format

---

## 2. Reviewer LLM Capabilities & Tool Access

**The reviewer gets a TEXT-ONLY prompt with NO tool use or function calling.**

Evidence:
- Search of review execution paths shows no anthropic SDK calls or tool definitions
- The external.py explicitly builds a **markdown launch prompt** for Claude to paste into a context-isolated session
- The output contract is **fixed JSON** that the reviewer must construct by hand:

```json
{
  "assessments": {"dimension": 0-100},
  "dimension_notes": {
    "dimension": {
      "evidence": ["observations"],
      "impact_scope": "local|module|subsystem|codebase",
      "fix_scope": "single_edit|multi_file_refactor|architectural_change",
      "confidence": "high|medium|low",
      "sub_axes": {abstraction-specific only}
    }
  },
  "dimension_judgment": {
    "dimension": {
      "strengths": ["0-5 things"],
      "issue_character": "one sentence",
      "score_rationale": "2-3 sentences"
    }
  },
  "issues": [{
    "dimension": "str",
    "identifier": "short_id",
    "summary": "one-liner",
    "related_files": ["paths"],
    "evidence": ["observations"],
    "suggestion": "fix recommendation",
    "confidence": "high|medium|low",
    "impact_scope": "local|module|subsystem|codebase",
    "fix_scope": "single_edit|multi_file_refactor|architectural_change",
    "root_cause_cluster": "optional"
  }],
  "retrospective": {
    "root_causes": ["optional"],
    "likely_symptoms": ["optional"],
    "possible_false_positives": ["optional"]
  }
}
```

The reviewer must:
1. Read the blind packet JSON (contains `holistic_context` with all scan evidence)
2. Read seed files from the batch
3. Explore the repository at will
4. Return only valid JSON (no markdown fences)

---

## 3. Mechanical Evidence Presentation

The `gather_mechanical_evidence()` output is presented in **two ways**:

**In the blind packet** (as JSON):
```
"holistic_context": {
  "scan_evidence": {
    "complexity_hotspots": [
      {
        "file": "path.py",
        "loc": 1200,
        "complexity_score": 45.0,
        "signals": ["12 params", "nesting depth 5"],
        "component_count": 7,
        "function_count": 34,
        "monster_functions": 2,
        "cyclomatic_hotspots": 3
      }
    ],
    "error_hotspots": [...],
    ...
  },
  "abstractions": {
    "complexity_hotspots": [...]  // also enriched from evidence
  },
  "errors": {
    "exception_hotspots": [...],
    "mutable_globals": [...]
  },
  "coupling": {
    "boundary_violations": [...]
  },
  ...
}
```

**In the prompt text** (as guidance):
- Dimension-specific focus blocks guide where to look:
  - `abstraction_fitness`: delegation_density, typed_dict_violations, facade modules
  - `design_coherence`: signal_density (files where multiple detectors fired)
  - `cross_module_architecture`: boundary_violations, deferred_import_density
  - etc. (lines 50-83 in `prompt_sections.py`)

**Also in prompt**: 
- "Mechanical concern signals" section (up to 8 entries, best-effort from generated concern hypotheses)
- "Mechanical scan evidence" note (navigation aid, not scoring evidence)

---

## 4. Review Results Import Flow

**Concern verdict handling** (`desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/holistic_issue_flow.py`):

Issues can come back with three states:
1. **Regular review issues** (`concern_verdict` absent): detector="review"
2. **Confirmed concerns** (`concern_verdict: "confirmed"`): detector="concerns" + concern metadata
3. **Dismissed concerns** (`concern_verdict: "dismissed"`): fingerprint + reasoning stored in `concern_dismissals`

**Import pipeline** (`desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/holistic.py`):

```
parse_holistic_import_payload()
  ↓
_validate_and_build_issues()
  ├─ Regular issues → make_issue() with detector="review"
  ├─ Confirmed concerns → make_issue() with detector="concerns" + concern detail
  └─ Dismissed concerns → stored in state.concern_dismissals
  ↓
merge_scan() // Merges new issues into state
  ↓
_auto_resolve_stale_holistic() // Auto-resolves open holistic issues not in latest import
  ↓
store_assessments() // Stores dimension scores if present
  ├─ Validates against allowed dimensions
  ├─ Computes confidence weights
  └─ Updates state.assessments
```

**Concern dismissal metadata** stored per fingerprint:
```python
{
  "dismissed_at": timestamp,
  "reasoning": str,
  "concern_type": str,
  "concern_file": str,
  "source_issue_ids": [list of originating detector issues]
}
```

**Stale resolution**: When reimporting a partial scope (scoped_reimport=True), only dismisses issues from dimensions that were explicitly reimported.

---

## Summary: The Presentation Layer

**What the reviewer actually sees:**

1. **Blind packet JSON** (read via text editor): Contains full `holistic_context` with all scan evidence clusters as nested dicts
2. **Markdown launch prompt**: Text-only with rendered dimension rubrics, seed files, mechanical concern signals (8 capped), task requirements
3. **Repository files**: Can freely explore; no tools or special access
4. **Output template JSON**: Pre-filled session/dimension stubs; reviewer fills in JSON by hand

**No tool use**: The reviewer is purely a text/JSON interface. They receive structured data, read code, and return structured decisions. The system is designed for **blind, unbiased scoring** with explicit scope controls (exclusions, suppress lists, zone overrides).

The mechanical evidence acts as a **navigation aid**—starting points for where to look—but all scoring is qualitative based on the reviewer's code reading and the dimension rubrics.

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding. Let me create a summary document based on all the findings:

## Summary: Suppression & Wontfix Mechanisms in Desloppify

Based on my comprehensive search of the desloppify codebase, here's what currently exists for permanently marking issues as "not-a-problem":

---

### 1. **SUPPRESSION MECHANISM** (`suppressed` flag on issues)

**What it is:** The primary mechanism for permanently silencing issues through ignore patterns.

**Fields on `Issue` TypedDict:**
- `suppressed: NotRequired[bool]` — boolean flag
- `suppressed_at: NotRequired[str | None]` — timestamp when suppressed
- `suppression_pattern: NotRequired[str | None]` — the pattern that matched

**How it works:**
- Command: `desloppify suppress <pattern>` (at `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/suppress.py`)
- Pattern types: glob patterns, issue ID prefixes, file paths
- On match, issues get `suppressed = True`
- Suppressed issues are **completely invisible** to:
  - Scoring (excluded from `_count_issues()` in state_integration.py)
  - Work queue ranking
  - Narrative generation
  - Dimension calculations
- Score improves when issues are suppressed (they don't count as failures)
- Suppression is **idempotent** — scan can re-mark issues as suppressed if ignore patterns match

**Persistence:**
- Lives in state as part of the issue object
- Removed on `desloppify resolve` (when manually resolving to any status, fields are cleared)
- Can be re-applied if pattern matches on next scan

**Tests:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/state/test_state_suppression_and_integrity.py`

---

### 2. **WONTFIX STATUS** (issue `status` field)

**What it is:** A manual resolution status marking an issue as "we've decided not to fix this."

**Status enum values** (in `base/enums.py`):
- `OPEN = "open"`
- `FIXED = "fixed"`
- `WONTFIX = "wontfix"`  ← this one
- `FALSE_POSITIVE = "false_positive"`
- `AUTO_RESOLVED = "auto_resolved"`

**How to set it:**
- Command: `desloppify resolve <pattern> --status wontfix --note "reason"` (attestation required)
- Creates metadata on the issue:
  - `wontfix_scan_count` — snapshot of scan count when marked wontfix
  - `wontfix_snapshot` — frozen snapshot of: tier, confidence, detail (loc, complexity_score, etc.)

**Wontfix behavior:**
- **Does NOT hide** the issue from scoring (it still counts as a failure in scoring calculations)
- Issues with `status=wontfix` are excluded from the default work queue
- Manually resolved issues cannot be auto-resolved by scan (they stay wontfix until re-opened)

**Stale Wontfix Detection:** (in `app/commands/scan/wontfix.py`)
- Detector: `stale_wontfix` — synthetic detector that re-triage wontfix issues that are:
  - **Old** (haven't been reviewed in N scans — configurable `decay_scans`)
  - **Worsening** (structural complexity or LOC grew significantly)
- Creates new `stale_wontfix` issues in the queue to prompt re-triage
- Checks for severity drift in detail fields (complexity_score, loc)

**Tests:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/scan/test_scan_workflow_wontfix_direct.py`

---

### 3. **DISMISSED CONCERNS** (holistic review dismissals)

**What it is:** A separate mechanism for dismissing synthesized concerns (architectural patterns flagged by holistic review).

**Data structure:** `ConcernDismissal` TypedDict in schema_types_review.py:
```python
dismissed_at: str
reason: str | None
dimension: str
reasoning: str
concern_type: str
concern_file: str
source_issue_ids: list[str]  # ← key for re-detection prevention
```

**Stored in state as:** `concern_dismissals: dict[fingerprint -> ConcernDismissal]`

**How dismissals work:**
- During `desloppify review --import` (holistic mode), review output can include `concern_verdict: "dismissed"`
- Dismissals must include a `concern_fingerprint` (hash of concern type + file + detector keys)
- Stored in state keyed by fingerprint
- Concerns re-generated on next holistic review import

**Fingerprinting & Re-detection Prevention:**
- Fingerprint = SHA256 hash of: `f"{concern_type}::{file}::{','.join(sorted(key_signals))}"`[:16]
- `_is_dismissed()` checks: was this fingerprint dismissed AND source issue set hasn't changed?
- If source issues changed, concern is **not re-suppressed** (can surface again)
- `cleanup_stale_dismissals()` removes dismissals when all source issues disappear

**Key difference from suppression:** Dismissals prevent *concerns* (synthesized patterns), not individual issues. The underlying mechanical issues can still appear.

**Tests:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/tests/detectors/test_concerns.py`, `intelligence/test_review_import_prepare_split_direct.py`

---

### 4. **TRIAGE DISMISSALS** (epic/cluster triage dismissals)

**What it is:** Dismissals from the epic triage process (plan orchestration).

**Data structure:** `DismissedIssue` dataclass in `engine/_plan/triage/prompt.py`:
```python
issue_id: str
reason: str
```

**Stored in:** `skipped` metadata under plan, not in main state issues

**How they work:**
- During triage, the LLM can flag issues as `dismissed_issues` (false positives, not worth triaging)
- Dismissed issues are moved out of the work queue
- Stored with `triaged_out` kind with metadata:
  - `reason` — why dismissed
  - `skipped_at_scan` — scan count when dismissed
  - `review_after` — optional future re-review date (not yet implemented?)

**Persistence:** Stored in plan's `skipped` dict, not in main state. **Does NOT persist across plans.**

**Tests:** `tests/plan/test_epic_triage_apply.py` (many test cases)

---

### 5. **GAPS & WHAT DOESN'T EXIST**

**What's missing:**

1. **No persistent fingerprinting for individual issues** — only for concerns (holistic patterns)
   - If you dismiss an individual issue in triage, it won't automatically be re-dismissed on the next triage
   - Triage dismissals live only in the plan, not in state

2. **No "defer" status** — wontfix is "decision made," but no way to say "revisit this in N months"
   - `review_after` field exists in triage dismissal schema but isn't implemented
   - Reminders can flag old wontfix, but no automatic re-queue

3. **No "snooze" mechanism** — can't temporarily hide an issue without suppressing it
   - Suppression = permanent (until pattern removed)
   - Wontfix = counted in scoring as failure (not hidden)

4. **Suppression and wontfix are orthogonal** — an issue can be both
   - `suppressed = True` hides from scoring
   - `status = "wontfix"` keeps it visible in UI but out of default queue
   - No built-in semantics tying them together

5. **No "dismiss for this file/module" convenience** — only exact patterns
   - Must use full glob/ID patterns; no "suppress all issues in this directory" shorthand (though `dir/*` would work)

6. **No "mark dismissed across all scans"** — dismissed concerns are checked on each holistic import
   - If source issues change (new files added, old removed), concern may surface again
   - Design by intention (catch new patterns)

---

### 6. **SCORING & VISIBILITY RULES**

**Suppressed issues:**
- Excluded from `_count_issues()` — don't contribute to stats
- Excluded from scoring candidates in `_iter_scoring_candidates()`
- **Score improves** when suppressed
- Invisible in narrative, reminders, work queue

**Wontfix issues:**
- Counted in stats (as resolved)
- **Count as failures in scoring** (still contribute to weighted failures)
- Excluded from default work queue
- Show up in "open debt" reporting
- Re-trigger "stale_wontfix" issues if old/worsening

**Dismissed concerns:**
- Don't generate new concern issues on re-import (if source issues unchanged)
- Can resurface if source issues change

**Dismissed triage issues:**
- Moved out of queue for current plan
- **NOT persisted** — will re-appear in next triage

---

### 7. **KEY FILES**

- **Suppress command:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/suppress.py`
- **Wontfix helpers:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/scan/wontfix.py`
- **Issue schema:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/schema_types_issues.py`
- **Resolution (wontfix/fixed):** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/resolution.py`
- **Merge/upsert logic:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/merge_issues.py`
- **Filtering & suppression:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_state/filtering.py`
- **Holistic concerns:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_concerns/generators.py`
- **Holistic dismissals:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/holistic_issue_flow.py`
- **Triage dismissals:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/engine/_plan/triage/dismiss.py`
- **Enums:** `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/base/enums.py`

---

### 8. **RECOMMENDATION SUMMARY**

For "mark this as not-a-problem permanently":

1. **Short-term (scan-to-scan):** Use `desloppify suppress <pattern>` to hide permanently + improve score
2. **Medium-term (track decision):** Use `desloppify resolve <pattern> --status wontfix` to record decision but keep visible in reports
3. **Holistic patterns:** Use review dismissals (in `desloppify review --import`) with fingerprinting to prevent re-surfacing
4. **Plan-scoped:** Triage dismissals only work within a single plan

**Gaps to address in future:**
- Persistent fingerprinting for individual issues (not just concerns)
- "Defer" status or `review_after` scheduling
- "Snooze" mechanism separate from suppression
- Convenient shorthand for suppressing entire directories

Perfect! Now I have a comprehensive understanding. Let me create a detailed summary report:

## Finding Structure & Explorable Checklist Analysis

Based on my thorough exploration, here's what I found about judgment-required findings and how they could be presented as an explorable checklist:

### 1. **Cluster Builders & Data Granularity**

The `_build_*` functions in `desloppify/intelligence/review/context_holistic/_clusters_*.py` produce **aggregated, file-level or pattern-level evidence clusters**, NOT individual findings. Examples:

- **`_build_complexity_hotspots()`** → Top 20 files with composite complexity scores (file-level, aggregated metrics)
- **`_build_error_hotspots()`** → Files with 3+ exception handling issues, grouped by smell_id (file + aggregation)
- **`_build_boundary_violations()`** → Individual boundary crossing issues (more granular: file + target + direction)
- **`_build_duplicate_clusters()`** → Duplicate clusters grouped by kind, with file lists (cluster-level)
- **`_build_naming_drift()`** → Directories with naming convention outliers (directory + outlier file lists)

**Key insight**: These clusters are **detective summaries** meant for holistic review context, not individual findings. They lose individual issue granularity by design.

### 2. **Canonical Issue Structure (from `schema_types_issues.py`)**

The `Issue` TypedDict shows the canonical structure:

```python
class Issue(TypedDict):
    id: str                           # detector::file::name or detector::file
    detector: str                     # "smells", "structural", "coupling", etc.
    file: str                         # relative path
    tier: int                         # 1-5 priority level
    confidence: str                   # "high", "medium", "low"
    summary: str                      # human-readable summary
    detail: dict[str, Any]            # Detector-specific payload
    status: Status                    # "open", "fixed", "wontfix", etc.
    note: str | None                 # User annotation
    first_seen: str                   # ISO timestamp
    last_seen: str                    # ISO timestamp
    resolved_at: str | None           # ISO timestamp
    reopen_count: int
    suppressed: bool                  # optional
    zone: str                         # optional: production/test/config/vendor/etc
```

**Detail dict shapes vary by detector** (from schema comments):
- **`smells`**: `{smell_id, severity, count, lines: list[int]}`
- **`structural`**: `{loc, complexity_score?, signals?: list[str], name?}` 
- **`coupling`**: `{target, direction, sole_tool?, importer_count?}`
- **`naming`**: `{expected_convention}`
- **`dupes`**: `{fn_a, fn_b, similarity, kind, cluster_size, cluster: list}`
- **`review`** (judgment): `{holistic?, dimension?, related_files: list[str], suggestion?, evidence: list[str]}`

### 3. **Judgment-Required Detectors**

These have `needs_judgment=True` in the registry:

```
smells, orphaned, uncalled_functions, flat_dirs, naming, single_use, coupling, 
cycles, facade, structural, props, react, dupes, patterns, dict_keys, signature, 
global_mutable_config, private_imports, layer_violation, responsibility_cohesion, 
boilerplate_duplication, concerns (design concerns)
```

All of these emit individual **findings** (per-file, per-issue), not aggregated clusters.

### 4. **Current "Show" Command Capabilities**

`desloppify show <detector>` already provides individual finding views:

- **Groups findings by file** (in `render_issues()`)
- **Shows per-issue detail** with:
  - Tier, confidence, summary
  - Formatted detail fields (lines, category, target, etc. from `DETAIL_DISPLAY`)
  - Optional code snippet (`--code` flag)
  - Reopen count warning (if ≥2)
  - User notes
  - Full issue ID
- **Renders to JSON** (`--output` flag) with structure: `{query, status_filter, total, summary: {by_tier, by_detector, files}, by_file: {filepath: [issues]}}`
- **Applies noise budgets** (respects detector-level thresholds)

### 5. **Backlog Command**

`desloppify backlog` shows items **not currently in the execution queue**:
- Uses `build_backlog_queue()` which filters against the living plan
- Same rendering as `next` but excludes clustered/queued items
- Shows purely by priority outside of plan context

### 6. **Data Granularity: Is It Enough for a Checklist?**

**YES, the data IS granular enough**. Each judgment finding has:

- ✓ Stable ID (detector::file::name)
- ✓ File location (path + optional line numbers in detail)
- ✓ Severity tier (1-5)
- ✓ Confidence level
- ✓ Detector-specific detail payload
- ✓ Status tracking (open/fixed/wontfix)
- ✓ Ability to annotate with notes
- ✓ Zone classification (production vs test vs config)
- ✓ Reopen history

### 7. **What Would Be Needed for a Judgment Checklist**

To present individual findings as an **explorable one-by-one checklist for reviewer judgment**, you'd need to:

1. **Filter to judgment detectors only** (the 22-item list above)
2. **Sort by priority**: tier (lower better) → confidence (higher better) → detector priority
3. **Group optionally by**:
   - Detector type (all smells together, all structural issues together)
   - File or directory
   - Zone (production issues first)
   - Dimension impact (if stored in review detail)
4. **Present each finding with**:
   - Issue ID (clickable/copyable)
   - File + line(s)
   - Summary + detector-specific detail
   - Current status and note
   - Related files (if present in detail)
   - Evidence/signals (from detail.evidence or detail.signals)
5. **Track checklist state**:
   - Mark each as reviewed (status → fixed/wontfix or skip to next)
   - Collect judgments (accept/dispute detector confidence)
   - Aggregate findings by pattern (e.g., "this coupling issue shows a real pattern")

### 8. **Existing Facilities**

- **`next` command**: Already shows execution queue items one-at-a-time with context
- **`show <detector>` command**: Can filter to specific detectors
- **Query output**: Written to `.desloppify/query.json` for external tools
- **Living plan**: Tracks judgment issues in queue order + clusters
- **`resolve` command**: Already marks findings with judgments (fixed, wontfix, false_positive)

The **execution queue in the living plan** (`engine/_plan/`) actually tracks judgment issues in prioritized order with cluster groupings—this is the closest existing structure to a "reviewer's judgment checklist."
