1→"""Resolve command handlers."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→import desloppify.intelligence.narrative.core as narrative_mod
     8→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     9→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.attestation import (
    10→    show_note_length_requirement,
    11→    validate_note_length,
    12→)
    13→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.guardrails import require_triage_current_or_exit
    14→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import resolve_lang
    15→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.persist import save_state_or_exit
    16→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.queue_progress import show_score_with_plan_context
    17→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.state import state_path
    18→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    19→from desloppify.engine._state.resolution import coerce_assessment_score
    20→
    21→from .apply import _resolve_all_patterns, _write_resolve_query_entry
    22→from .living_plan import update_living_plan_after_resolve
    23→from .messages import print_fixed_next_user_message, print_no_match_warning
    24→from .queue_guard import _check_queue_order_guard
    25→from .render import (
    26→    _print_next_command,
    27→    _print_resolve_summary,
    28→    _print_subjective_reset_hint,
    29→    _print_wontfix_batch_warning,
    30→    render_commit_guidance,
    31→)
    32→from .selection import (
    33→    ResolveQueryContext,
    34→    _enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation,
    35→    _previous_score_snapshot,
    36→    _validate_resolve_inputs,
    37→)
    38→
    39→
    40→def _validate_fixed_note(args: argparse.Namespace) -> bool:
    41→    if args.status != "fixed":
    42→        return True
    43→    note = getattr(args, "note", None)
    44→    if validate_note_length(note):
    45→        return True
    46→    show_note_length_requirement(note)
    47→    return False
    48→
    49→
    50→def _load_state_with_guards(
    51→    args: argparse.Namespace,
    52→    *,
    53→    attestation: str | None,
    54→) -> tuple[str, dict] | None:
    55→    """Validate inputs, apply guardrails, and load state for resolve."""
    56→    _validate_resolve_inputs(args, attestation)
    57→    if not _validate_fixed_note(args):
    58→        return None
    59→
    60→    state_file = state_path(args)
    61→    state = state_mod.load_state(state_file)
    62→
    63→    if _check_queue_order_guard(state, args.patterns, args.status):
    64→        return None
    65→
    66→    if args.status == "fixed":
    67→        require_triage_current_or_exit(
    68→            state=state,
    69→            bypass=bool(getattr(args, "force_resolve", False)),
    70→            attest=getattr(args, "attest", "") or "",
    71→        )
    72→
    73→    return state_file, state
    74→
    75→
    76→def _resolve_ids_with_snapshots(
    77→    state: dict,
    78→    args: argparse.Namespace,
    79→    *,
    80→    attestation: str | None,
    81→) -> tuple[object, dict[str, float], list[str]]:
    82→    """Apply resolve patterns and return pre/post context for rendering."""
    83→    _enforce_batch_wontfix_confirmation(
    84→        state,
    85→        args,
    86→        attestation=attestation,
    87→        resolve_all_patterns_fn=_resolve_all_patterns,
    88→    )
    89→
    90→    prev = _previous_score_snapshot(state)
    91→    prev_subjective_scores = {
    92→        str(dim): (coerce_assessment_score(payload) or 0.0)
    93→        for dim, payload in (state.get("subjective_assessments") or {}).items()
    94→        if isinstance(dim, str)
    95→    }
    96→    all_resolved = _resolve_all_patterns(state, args, attestation=attestation)
    97→    return prev, prev_subjective_scores, all_resolved
    98→
    99→
   100→def cmd_resolve(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
   101→    """Resolve issue(s) matching one or more patterns."""
   102→    attestation = getattr(args, "attest", None)
   103→    loaded = _load_state_with_guards(args, attestation=attestation)
   104→    if loaded is None:
   105→        return
   106→    state_file, state = loaded
   107→    prev, prev_subjective_scores, all_resolved = _resolve_ids_with_snapshots(
   108→        state,
   109→        args,
   110→        attestation=attestation,
   111→    )
   112→    if not all_resolved:
   113→        print_no_match_warning(args)
   114→        return
   115→
   116→    save_state_or_exit(state, state_file)
   117→
   118→    plan, cluster_ctx = update_living_plan_after_resolve(
   119→        args=args,
   120→        all_resolved=all_resolved,
   121→        attestation=attestation,
   122→        state_file=state_file,
   123→    )
   124→    mid_cluster = (
   125→        cluster_ctx.cluster_name is not None and not cluster_ctx.cluster_completed
   126→    )
   127→
   128→    _print_resolve_summary(status=args.status, all_resolved=all_resolved)
   129→    _print_wontfix_batch_warning(
   130→        state,
   131→        status=args.status,
   132→        resolved_count=len(all_resolved),
   133→    )
   134→    show_score_with_plan_context(state, prev)
   135→    if not mid_cluster:
   136→        render_commit_guidance(state, plan, all_resolved, args.status)
   137→    _print_subjective_reset_hint(
   138→        args=args,
   139→        state=state,
   140→        all_resolved=all_resolved,
   141→        prev_subjective_scores=prev_subjective_scores,
   142→    )
   143→
   144→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
   145→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
   146→    narrative = narrative_mod.compute_narrative(
   147→        state,
   148→        context=narrative_mod.NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="resolve"),
   149→    )
   150→    if narrative.get("milestone"):
   151→        print(colorize(f"  → {narrative['milestone']}", "green"))
   152→
   153→    next_command = _print_next_command(state)
   154→    _write_resolve_query_entry(
   155→        ResolveQueryContext(
   156→            patterns=args.patterns,
   157→            status=args.status,
   158→            resolved=all_resolved,
   159→            next_command=next_command,
   160→            prev_overall=prev.overall,
   161→            prev_objective=prev.objective,
   162→            prev_strict=prev.strict,
   163→            prev_verified=prev.verified,
   164→            attestation=attestation,
   165→            narrative=narrative,
   166→            state=state,
   167→        )
   168→    )
   169→    print_fixed_next_user_message(
   170→        args=args,
   171→        plan=plan,
   172→        next_command=next_command,
   173→        mid_cluster=mid_cluster,
   174→        cluster_ctx=cluster_ctx,
   175→    )
   176→
   177→
   178→__all__ = ["_check_queue_order_guard", "cmd_resolve"]

    20→def _try_expand_cluster(pattern: str) -> list[str] | None:
    21→    """If pattern matches a cluster name, return its issue IDs."""
    22→    plan_status = resolve_plan_load_status()
    23→    if plan_status.degraded:
    24→        _logger.debug(
    25→            "cluster expansion skipped for %r due to degraded plan load (%s)",
    26→            pattern,
    27→            plan_status.error_kind,
    28→        )
    29→        warn_plan_load_degraded_once(
    30→            error_kind=plan_status.error_kind,
    31→            behavior=(
    32→                "Cluster-name pattern expansion is disabled until plan loading succeeds."
    33→            ),
    34→        )
    35→        return None
    36→    plan = plan_status.plan
    37→    if not isinstance(plan, dict):
    38→        return None
    39→    cluster = plan.get("clusters", {}).get(pattern)
    40→    if cluster and cluster.get("issue_ids"):
    41→        return list(cluster["issue_ids"])
    42→    return None
    43→
    44→
    45→def _resolve_all_patterns(

    58→                    state,

    60→                    args.status,

1→"""Top-level batch building APIs for holistic review preparation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from pathlib import Path
     6→
     7→from .prepare_batches_collectors import _DIMENSION_FILE_MAPPING, _FILE_COLLECTORS
     8→from .prepare_batches_core import (
     9→    _collect_files_from_batches,
    10→    _ensure_holistic_context,
    11→    _normalize_file_path,
    12→)
    13→
    14→
    15→def build_investigation_batches(
    16→    holistic_ctx,
    17→    lang: object,
    18→    *,
    19→    repo_root: Path | None = None,
    20→    max_files_per_batch: int | None = None,
    21→) -> list[dict]:
    22→    """Build one batch per dimension from holistic context."""
    23→    ctx = _ensure_holistic_context(holistic_ctx)
    24→    del lang
    25→    del repo_root
    26→
    27→    file_cache: dict[str, list[str]] = {}
    28→    batches: list[dict] = []
    29→
    30→    for dimension, collector_key in _DIMENSION_FILE_MAPPING.items():
    31→        if collector_key not in file_cache:
    32→            collector = _FILE_COLLECTORS[collector_key]
    33→            file_cache[collector_key] = collector(
    34→                ctx,
    35→                max_files=max_files_per_batch,
    36→            )
    37→
    38→        files = file_cache[collector_key]
    39→        if not files:
    40→            continue
    41→
    42→        batches.append(
    43→            {
    44→                "name": dimension,
    45→                "dimensions": [dimension],
    46→                "files_to_read": files,
    47→                "why": f"seed files for {dimension} review",
    48→            }
    49→        )
    50→
    51→    return batches
    52→
    53→
    54→def filter_batches_to_dimensions(
    55→    batches: list[dict],
    56→    dimensions: list[str],
    57→    *,
    58→    fallback_max_files: int | None = 80,
    59→) -> list[dict]:
    60→    """Keep only batches whose dimension is in the active set."""
    61→    selected = [dimension for dimension in dimensions if isinstance(dimension, str) and dimension]
    62→    if not selected:
    63→        return []
    64→    selected_set = set(selected)
    65→    filtered: list[dict] = []
    66→    covered: set[str] = set()
    67→    for batch in batches:
    68→        batch_dims = [dim for dim in batch.get("dimensions", []) if dim in selected_set]
    69→        if not batch_dims:
    70→            continue
    71→        filtered.append({**batch, "dimensions": batch_dims})
    72→        covered.update(batch_dims)
    73→
    74→    missing = [dim for dim in selected if dim not in covered]
    75→    if not missing:
    76→        return filtered
    77→
    78→    max_files = fallback_max_files if isinstance(fallback_max_files, int) else None
    79→    if isinstance(max_files, int) and max_files <= 0:
    80→        max_files = None

100→def batch_concerns(
   101→    concerns: list,
   102→    *,
   103→    max_files: int | None = None,
   104→    active_dimensions: list[str] | None = None,
   105→) -> dict | None:
   106→    """Build investigation batch from mechanical concern signals."""
   107→    del active_dimensions
   108→    if not concerns:
   109→        return None
   110→
   111→    types = sorted({concern.type for concern in concerns if concern.type})
   112→    why_parts = ["mechanical detectors identified structural patterns needing judgment"]
   113→    if types:
   114→        why_parts.append(f"concern types: {', '.join(types)}")
   115→
   116→    files: list[str] = []
   117→    seen: set[str] = set()
   118→    concern_signals: list[dict[str, object]] = []
   119→    for concern in concerns:
   120→        candidate = _normalize_file_path(getattr(concern, "file", ""))
   121→        if not candidate or candidate in seen:
   122→            continue
   123→        seen.add(candidate)
   124→        files.append(candidate)
   125→
   126→        evidence_raw = getattr(concern, "evidence", ())
   127→        evidence = [
   128→            str(entry).strip()
   129→            for entry in evidence_raw
   130→            if isinstance(entry, str) and entry.strip()
   131→        ][:4]
   132→        summary = str(getattr(concern, "summary", "")).strip()
   133→        question = str(getattr(concern, "question", "")).strip()
   134→        concern_type = str(getattr(concern, "type", "")).strip()
   135→        concern_signals.append(
   136→            {
   137→                "type": concern_type or "design_concern",
   138→                "file": candidate,
   139→                "summary": summary or "Mechanical concern requires subjective judgment",
   140→                "question": question or "Is this pattern intentional or debt?",
   141→                "evidence": evidence,
   142→            }
   143→        )
   144→
   145→    total_candidate_files = len(files)
   146→    if (
   147→        max_files is not None
   148→        and isinstance(max_files, int)
   149→        and max_files > 0
   150→        and total_candidate_files > max_files
   151→    ):
   152→        files = files[:max_files]
   153→        why_parts.append(
   154→            f"truncated to {max_files} files from {total_candidate_files} candidates"
   155→        )
   156→
   157→    return {
   158→        "name": "design_coherence",
   159→        "dimensions": ["design_coherence"],
   160→        "files_to_read": files,
   161→        "why": "; ".join(why_parts),
   162→        "total_candidate_files": total_candidate_files,
   163→        "concern_signals": concern_signals[:12],
   164→        "concern_signal_count": len(concern_signals),
   165→    }
   166→
   167→
   168→__all__ = ["batch_concerns", "build_investigation_batches", "filter_batches_to_dimensions"]

1→"""Small utility helpers for concern generation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import hashlib
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from .types import ConcernSignals, SignalKey
     9→
    10→
    11→def _update_max_signal(signals: ConcernSignals, key: SignalKey, value: object) -> None:
    12→    """Update numeric signal key with max(existing, value) when value is valid."""
    13→    if isinstance(value, bool) or not isinstance(value, int | float) or value <= 0:
    14→        return
    15→    current = float(signals.get(key, 0.0))
    16→    signals[key] = max(current, float(value))
    17→
    18→
    19→def _fingerprint(concern_type: str, file: str, key_signals: tuple[str, ...]) -> str:
    20→    """Stable hash of (type, file, sorted key signals)."""
    21→    raw = f"{concern_type}::{file}::{','.join(sorted(key_signals))}"
    22→    return hashlib.sha256(raw.encode()).hexdigest()[:16]
    23→
    24→
    25→def _is_dismissed(
    26→    dismissals: dict[str, Any], fingerprint: str, source_issue_ids: tuple[str, ...]
    27→) -> bool:
    28→    """Check if a concern was previously dismissed and source issues unchanged."""
    29→    entry = dismissals.get(fingerprint)
    30→    if not isinstance(entry, dict):
    31→        return False
    32→    prev_sources = set(entry.get("source_issue_ids", []))
    33→    return prev_sources == set(source_issue_ids)
    34→
    35→
    36→__all__ = ["_fingerprint", "_is_dismissed", "_update_max_signal"]
    37→

1→"""Suppress command handler — permanently silence issues matching a pattern."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import argparse
     6→
     7→from desloppify import state as state_mod
     8→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.attestation import (
     9→    show_attestation_requirement,
    10→    validate_attestation,
    11→)
    12→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.lang import resolve_lang
    13→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.persist import (
    14→    save_config_or_exit,
    15→    save_state_or_exit,
    16→)
    17→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.query import write_query
    18→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.queue_progress import show_score_with_plan_context
    19→from desloppify.app.commands.helpers.runtime import command_runtime
    20→from desloppify.base import config as config_mod
    21→from desloppify.base.exception_sets import CommandError
    22→from desloppify.base.output.terminal import colorize
    23→from desloppify.base.tooling import check_config_staleness
    24→from desloppify.engine._work_queue.core import ATTEST_EXAMPLE
    25→import desloppify.intelligence.narrative.core as narrative_mod
    26→
    27→
    28→def cmd_suppress(args: argparse.Namespace) -> None:
    29→    """Suppress issues matching a pattern."""
    30→    attestation = getattr(args, "attest", None)
    31→    if not validate_attestation(attestation):
    32→        show_attestation_requirement("Suppress", attestation, ATTEST_EXAMPLE)
    33→        raise CommandError("Suppress requires a valid attestation.")
    34→
    35→    runtime = command_runtime(args)
    36→    state_file = runtime.state_path
    37→    state = runtime.state
    38→    prev = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    39→
    40→    config = runtime.config
    41→    config_mod.add_ignore_pattern(config, args.pattern)
    42→    config["needs_rescan"] = True
    43→    save_config_or_exit(config)
    44→
    45→    removed = state_mod.remove_ignored_issues(state, args.pattern)
    46→    state.setdefault("attestation_log", []).append(
    47→        {
    48→            "timestamp": state.get("last_scan"),
    49→            "command": "suppress",
    50→            "pattern": args.pattern,
    51→            "attestation": attestation,
    52→            "affected": removed,
    53→        }
    54→    )
    55→    save_state_or_exit(state, state_file)
    56→
    57→    print(colorize(f"Added suppress pattern: {args.pattern}", "green"))
    58→    if removed:
    59→        print(f"  Removed {removed} matching issues from state.")
    60→    config_warning = check_config_staleness(config)
    61→    if config_warning:
    62→        print(colorize(f"  {config_warning}", "yellow"))
    63→    show_score_with_plan_context(state, prev)
    64→
    65→    lang = resolve_lang(args)
    66→    lang_name = lang.name if lang else None
    67→    narrative = narrative_mod.compute_narrative(
    68→        state,
    69→        context=narrative_mod.NarrativeContext(lang=lang_name, command="suppress"),
    70→    )
    71→    scores = state_mod.score_snapshot(state)
    72→    write_query(
    73→        {
    74→            "command": "suppress",
    75→            "pattern": args.pattern,
    76→            "removed": removed,
    77→            "overall_score": scores.overall,
    78→            "objective_score": scores.objective,
    79→            "strict_score": scores.strict,
    80→            "verified_strict_score": scores.verified,
    81→            "attestation": attestation,
    82→            "narrative": narrative,
    83→        }
    84→    )
    85→
    86→
    87→__all__ = ["cmd_suppress"]
    88→

1→"""Shared subprocess runner helpers for codex batch execution."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→import os
     6→import shutil
     7→import sys
     8→from pathlib import Path
     9→
    10→from desloppify.app.commands.review.runner_process_impl.attempts import (
    11→    handle_early_attempt_return,
    12→    handle_failed_attempt,
    13→    handle_successful_attempt,
    14→    handle_timeout_or_stall,
    15→    resolve_retry_config,
    16→    run_batch_attempt,
    17→)
    18→from desloppify.app.commands.review.runner_process_impl.io import extract_payload_from_log
    19→from desloppify.app.commands.review.runner_process_impl.types import (
    20→    CodexBatchRunnerDeps,
    21→    FollowupScanDeps,
    22→)
    23→
    24→
    25→def codex_batch_command(*, prompt: str, repo_root: Path, output_file: Path) -> list[str]:
    26→    """Build one codex exec command line for a batch prompt."""
    27→    effort = os.environ.get("DESLOPPIFY_CODEX_REASONING_EFFORT", "low").strip().lower()
    28→    if effort not in {"low", "medium", "high", "xhigh"}:
    29→        effort = "low"
    30→    codex = shutil.which("codex") or "codex"
    31→    return [
    32→        codex,
    33→        "exec",
    34→        "--ephemeral",
    35→        "-C",
    36→        str(repo_root),
    37→        "-s",
    38→        "workspace-write",
    39→        "-c",
    40→        'approval_policy="never"',
    41→        "-c",
    42→        f'model_reasoning_effort="{effort}"',
    43→        "-o",
    44→        str(output_file),
    45→        prompt,
    46→    ]
    47→
    48→
    49→def run_codex_batch(
    50→    *,
    51→    prompt: str,
    52→    repo_root: Path,
    53→    output_file: Path,
    54→    log_file: Path,
    55→    deps: CodexBatchRunnerDeps,
    56→    codex_batch_command_fn=None,
    57→) -> int:
    58→    """Execute one codex batch and return a stable CLI-style status code."""
    59→    if codex_batch_command_fn is None:
    60→        codex_batch_command_fn = codex_batch_command
    61→    cmd = codex_batch_command_fn(
    62→        prompt=prompt,
    63→        repo_root=repo_root,
    64→        output_file=output_file,
    65→    )
    66→    config = resolve_retry_config(deps)
    67→    log_sections: list[str] = []
    68→
    69→    for attempt in range(1, config.max_attempts + 1):
    70→        header, result = run_batch_attempt(
    71→            cmd=cmd,
    72→            deps=deps,
    73→            output_file=output_file,
    74→            log_file=log_file,
    75→            log_sections=log_sections,
    76→            attempt=attempt,
    77→            max_attempts=config.max_attempts,
    78→            use_popen=config.use_popen,
    79→            live_log_interval=config.live_log_interval,
    80→            stall_seconds=config.stall_seconds,
    81→        )
    82→        early_return = handle_early_attempt_return(result)
    83→        if early_return is not None:
    84→            return early_return
    85→        timeout_or_stall = handle_timeout_or_stall(
    86→            header=header,
    87→            result=result,
    88→            deps=deps,
    89→            output_file=output_file,
    90→            log_file=log_file,
    91→            log_sections=log_sections,
    92→            stall_seconds=config.stall_seconds,
    93→        )
    94→        if timeout_or_stall is not None:
    95→            if timeout_or_stall == 0:
    96→                return 0  # recovered from timeout/stall
    97→            # Non-recovered timeout/stall: retry if attempts remain
    98→            if attempt < config.max_attempts:
    99→                delay = config.retry_backoff_seconds * (2 ** (attempt - 1))
   100→                log_sections.append(
   101→                    f"Timeout/stall on attempt {attempt}/{config.max_attempts}; "
   102→                    f"retrying in {delay:.1f}s."
   103→                )
   104→                if delay > 0:
   105→                    deps.sleep_fn(delay)
   106→                continue
   107→            return timeout_or_stall
   108→
   109→        log_sections.append(
   110→            f"{header}\n\nSTDOUT:\n{result.stdout_text}\n\nSTDERR:\n{result.stderr_text}\n"
   111→        )
   112→
   113→        success_code = handle_successful_attempt(
   114→            result=result,
   115→            output_file=output_file,
   116→            log_file=log_file,
   117→            deps=deps,
   118→            log_sections=log_sections,
   119→        )
   120→        if success_code is not None:
   121→            return success_code
   122→        failure_code = handle_failed_attempt(
   123→            result=result,
   124→            deps=deps,
   125→            attempt=attempt,
   126→            max_attempts=config.max_attempts,
   127→            retry_backoff_seconds=config.retry_backoff_seconds,
   128→            log_file=log_file,
   129→            log_sections=log_sections,
   130→        )
   131→        if failure_code is not None:
   132→            return failure_code
   133→
   134→    deps.safe_write_text_fn(log_file, "\n\n".join(log_sections))
   135→    return 1
   136→
   137→
   138→def run_followup_scan(
   139→    *,
   140→    lang_name: str,
   141→    scan_path: str,
   142→    deps: FollowupScanDeps,
   143→    force_queue_bypass: bool = False,
   144→) -> int:
   145→    """Run a follow-up scan and return a non-zero status when it fails."""
   146→    scan_cmd = [
   147→        deps.python_executable,
   148→        "-m",
   149→        "desloppify",
   150→        "--lang",
   151→        lang_name,
   152→        "scan",
   153→        "--path",
   154→        scan_path,
   155→    ]
   156→    if force_queue_bypass:
   157→        followup_attest = (
   158→            "I understand this is not the intended workflow and "
   159→            "I am intentionally skipping queue completion"
   160→        )
   161→        scan_cmd.extend(["--force-rescan", "--attest", followup_attest])
   162→        print(
   163→            deps.colorize_fn(
   164→                "  Follow-up scan queue bypass enabled (--force-followup-scan).",
   165→                "yellow",
   166→            )
   167→        )
   168→    print(deps.colorize_fn("\n  Running follow-up scan...", "bold"))
   169→    try:
   170→        result = deps.subprocess_run(
   171→            scan_cmd,
   172→            cwd=str(deps.project_root),
   173→            timeout=deps.timeout_seconds,
   174→        )
   175→    except deps.timeout_error:
   176→        print(
   177→            deps.colorize_fn(
   178→                f"  Follow-up scan timed out after {deps.timeout_seconds}s.",
   179→                "yellow",
   180→            ),
   181→            file=sys.stderr,
   182→        )
   183→        return 124
   184→    except OSError as exc:
   185→        print(
   186→            deps.colorize_fn(f"  Follow-up scan failed: {exc}", "red"),
   187→            file=sys.stderr,
   188→        )
   189→        return 1
   190→    return int(getattr(result, "returncode", 0) or 0)
   191→
   192→
   193→__all__ = [
   194→    "CodexBatchRunnerDeps",
   195→    "FollowupScanDeps",
   196→    "extract_payload_from_log",
   197→    "codex_batch_command",
   198→    "run_codex_batch",
   199→    "run_followup_scan",
   200→]
   201→

    29→from desloppify.app.cli_support.parser_groups_plan_impl import add_plan_parser
    30→
    31→USAGE_EXAMPLES = """
    32→workflow:
    33→  scan       Run detectors, update state, show diff
    34→  status     Score dashboard with dimension health
    35→  next       Show the next execution item from the living plan
    36→  backlog    Show broader backlog items not currently in execution
    37→  plan       Living plan: prioritize, cluster, resolve, skip, annotate
    38→
    39→investigate:
    40→  show       Dig into issues by file/dir/detector/ID
    41→  tree       Annotated codebase tree (zoom with --focus)
    42→  viz        Interactive HTML treemap
    43→  detect     Run a single detector directly (bypass state)
    44→
    45→improve:
    46→  autofix    Auto-fix mechanical issues
    47→  suppress   Suppress issues matching a pattern
    48→  exclude    Exclude path pattern from scanning
    49→  move       Move file/dir and update import references
    50→  review     Holistic subjective review (LLM-based)
    51→
    52→configure:
    53→  zone       Show/set zone classifications
    54→  config     Project configuration
    55→  langs      List language plugins
    56→  dev        Developer utilities
    57→  update-skill  Install/update agent skill document
    58→
    59→examples:
    60→  desloppify scan
    61→  desloppify scan --skip-slow --profile ci
    62→  desloppify plan                        # full prioritized markdown
    63→  desloppify plan queue                  # compact table of execution items
    64→  desloppify next --count 10             # top 10 execution items
    65→  desloppify backlog --count 10          # top 10 backlog items
    66→  desloppify show src/components/Modal.tsx
    67→  desloppify plan resolve "unused::src/foo.tsx::React" \\
    68→    --note "removed import" --attest "I have actually ..."
    69→  desloppify review --run-batches --parallel --scan-after-import
    70→"""
    71→
    72→
    73→class _NoAbbrevArgumentParser(argparse.ArgumentParser):
    74→    """Argparse parser variant that disables long-option abbreviation."""
    75→
    76→    def __init__(self, *args, **kwargs):
    77→        kwargs.setdefault("allow_abbrev", False)
    78→        super().__init__(*args, **kwargs)
    79→
    80→
    81→def _cli_version_string() -> str:
    82→    """Return the best available CLI version label."""
    83→    try:
    84→        return f"desloppify {get_version('desloppify')}"
    85→    except PackageNotFoundError:
    86→        return "desloppify (version unknown)"
    87→
    88→
    89→def create_parser(*, langs: list[str], detector_names: list[str]) -> argparse.ArgumentParser:
    90→    """Build top-level CLI parser with all subcommands."""
    91→    lang_help = ", ".join(langs) if langs else "registered languages"
    92→
    93→    parser = _NoAbbrevArgumentParser(
    94→        prog="desloppify",
    95→        description="Desloppify — codebase health tracker",
    96→        epilog=USAGE_EXAMPLES,
    97→        formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter,
    98→    )
    99→    parser.add_argument(
   100→        "--lang",
   101→        type=str,
   102→        default=None,
   103→        help=f"Language to scan ({lang_help}). Auto-detected if omitted.",
   104→    )
   105→    parser.add_argument(
   106→        "--exclude",
   107→        action="append",
   108→        default=None,
   109→        metavar="PATTERN",
   110→        help="Path pattern to exclude (component/prefix match; repeatable)",
   111→    )
   112→    parser.add_argument(
   113→        "--version",
   114→        "-V",
   115→        action="version",
   116→        version=_cli_version_string(),
   117→    )
   118→    sub = parser.add_subparsers(
   119→        dest="command",
   120→        parser_class=_NoAbbrevArgumentParser,
   121→    )
   122→    # workflow
   123→    _add_scan_parser(sub)
   124→    _add_status_parser(sub)
   125→    _add_next_parser(sub)
   126→    _add_backlog_parser(sub)
   127→    add_plan_parser(sub)
   128→    # investigate
   129→    _add_show_parser(sub)
   130→    _add_tree_parser(sub)
   131→    _add_viz_parser(sub)
   132→    _add_detect_parser(sub, detector_names)
   133→    # improve
   134→    _add_autofix_parser(sub, langs)
   135→    _add_suppress_parser(sub)
   136→    _add_exclude_parser(sub)
   137→    _add_move_parser(sub)
   138→    _add_review_parser(sub)
   139→    # configure
   140→    _add_zone_parser(sub)
   141→    _add_config_parser(sub)
   142→    _add_langs_parser(sub)
   143→    _add_dev_parser(sub)
   144→    _add_update_skill_parser(sub)
   145→    return parser
   146→
   147→
   148→__all__ = ["USAGE_EXAMPLES", "create_parser"]
   149→

1→"""State readers for concern generation."""
     2→
     3→from __future__ import annotations
     4→
     5→from collections import defaultdict
     6→from typing import Any
     7→
     8→from desloppify.engine._state.schema import StateModel
     9→
    10→
    11→def _open_issues(state: StateModel) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
    12→    """Return all open issues from state."""
    13→    issues = state.get("issues", {})
    14→    return [
    15→        finding for finding in issues.values()
    16→        if isinstance(finding, dict) and finding.get("status") == "open"
    17→    ]
    18→
    19→
    20→def _group_by_file(state: StateModel) -> dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]]:
    21→    """Group open issues by file, excluding holistic (file='.') issues."""
    22→    by_file: dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]] = defaultdict(list)
    23→    for finding in _open_issues(state):
    24→        file = finding.get("file", "")
    25→        if file and file != ".":
    26→            by_file[file].append(finding)
    27→    return dict(by_file)
    28→
    29→
    30→__all__ = ["_group_by_file", "_open_issues"]
    31→

## Implementation Plan: Reviewer Finding Exploration and Adjudication

### Key Design Decision: Extend the Output JSON, Not CLI Commands

After exploring the codebase, the simplest approach is a hybrid:

1. **Use the existing `desloppify show` command** for the reviewer to query findings on-demand (it already supports detector-based filtering, JSON output, and file-scoped queries).
2. **Extend the review output JSON** to include per-finding verdicts alongside the existing `issues[]` array.
3. **Use the existing `concern_dismissals` mechanism** for persistence, extended slightly to handle raw finding dismissals (not just generated concern dismissals).

This avoids adding new CLI commands entirely. The reviewer already has `workspace-write` access and can run `desloppify show smells --output /tmp/smells.json` to get structured JSON for any detector.

### Architecture Overview

The flow becomes:

1. **Prompt tells reviewer** about judgment findings and the CLI commands to explore them
2. **Reviewer queries** individual detectors via `desloppify show <detector> --output /tmp/findings.json` and reads actual source code
3. **Reviewer records verdicts** in a new `finding_verdicts[]` array in the output JSON
4. **Import pipeline processes** verdicts: confirmed -> becomes `concerns::` issue, dismissed -> stored in `concern_dismissals`, skip -> ignored

### Step-by-Step Implementation

#### Step 1: Extend the review prompt to include finding exploration instructions

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/prompt_sections.py`**

Add a new `render_judgment_findings_instructions()` function, following the pattern of `render_mechanical_concern_signals()`. This section would:

- List the judgment-required detectors that have open findings in this batch's scope
- Show per-detector counts (e.g., "smells: 12 findings, structural: 4 findings, naming: 8 findings")
- Provide exact CLI commands to query them:
  ```
  desloppify show smells --output /tmp/smells.json --no-budget
  desloppify show structural --output /tmp/structural.json --no-budget
  desloppify show naming --output /tmp/naming.json --no-budget
  ```
- Explain the verdict protocol: for each finding you investigate, record a verdict in `finding_verdicts[]`

The `--no-budget` flag is important -- it disables the noise budget that normally caps how many findings per detector are shown, so the reviewer sees them all.

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/prompt_template.py`**

Wire `render_judgment_findings_instructions()` into `render_batch_prompt()`, inserted after `render_mechanical_concern_signals(batch)`. Only render this section when the batch includes dimensions that benefit from finding-level adjudication (design_coherence, convention_outlier, etc.).

#### Step 2: Extend the output JSON schema to include `finding_verdicts`

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/prompt_template.py`**

In `_render_output_schema()`, add a new top-level array after `issues[]`:

```
"finding_verdicts": [{
    "finding_id": "structural::src/big_file.py",
    "verdict": "confirm|dismiss|skip",
    "reasoning": "brief explanation of judgment",
    "promoted_dimension": "design_coherence"  // required when verdict=confirm
}]
```

This is separate from `issues[]` because verdicts adjudicate existing mechanical findings, while `issues[]` reports newly-discovered defects. The reviewer can still use `issues[]` with `concern_verdict: "confirmed"` for generated concerns -- the two mechanisms are complementary.

#### Step 3: Add finding_verdicts to the import contracts

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/contracts_types.py`**

Add a `FindingVerdictPayload` TypedDict:

```python
class FindingVerdictPayload(TypedDict, total=False):
    finding_id: str       # stable issue ID from state
    verdict: str          # "confirm" | "dismiss" | "skip"
    reasoning: str
    promoted_dimension: str  # which dimension this promotes to (for confirm)
```

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/contracts_validation.py`**

Add `validate_finding_verdict_payload()` that checks:
- `finding_id` is non-empty
- `verdict` is one of the three allowed values
- `promoted_dimension` is present when verdict is "confirm"
- `reasoning` is present (can be brief)

#### Step 4: Process finding verdicts during import

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/holistic_issue_flow.py`**

Add a new function `process_finding_verdicts()`:

For **"confirm"** verdicts:
- Look up the finding by `finding_id` in `state["issues"]`
- Build a `concerns::` issue (reuse the existing `make_issue` pattern from `validate_and_build_issues` at line 144)
- The new concern issue gets `detector="concerns"`, `detail.concern_verdict="confirmed"`, etc.
- Return it as part of the review_issues list so it goes through the normal merge pipeline

For **"dismiss"** verdicts:
- Compute a fingerprint from the finding_id using the existing `_fingerprint()` utility from `desloppify/engine/_concerns/utils.py`
- Store in `state["concern_dismissals"]` with the finding_id as a source_issue_id
- This means if the same finding re-appears after rescan, the dismissal fingerprint (keyed on the stable issue ID components) will suppress it from being surfaced again in future reviews
- The existing `cleanup_stale_dismissals()` already handles removing dismissals when their source issues disappear, so this gets lifecycle management for free

For **"skip"** verdicts:
- No action needed -- the finding stays as-is and will be re-presented in the next review

#### Step 5: Wire finding verdicts into the holistic import orchestrator

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/holistic.py`**

In `import_holistic_review()`, after processing `dismissed_concerns` (around line 99), add a block to extract and process `finding_verdicts` from the review payload. Call `process_finding_verdicts()` and merge the resulting confirmed issues into `review_issues`, and store the resulting dismissals into `state["concern_dismissals"]`.

#### Step 6: Populate finding summaries in batch preparation

**File: `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/prepare_batches_builders.py`**

In `batch_concerns()` (line 100), extend the batch dict to include a `judgment_finding_summary` key containing per-detector counts and a sample of finding IDs. This data is used by the prompt renderer (Step 1) to tell the reviewer what's available without embedding every finding in the prompt.

Alternatively, this data can be computed at prompt-render time from state, which is simpler -- the prompt renderer already has access to the holistic context which includes scan evidence.

### Key Design Decisions Explained

**Why not new CLI commands?** The existing `desloppify show <detector>` already does exactly what the reviewer needs -- it filters issues by detector, shows details, and supports `--output` for JSON. Adding a dedicated `desloppify review adjudicate` command would be more API surface for marginal benefit.

**Why `finding_verdicts[]` separate from `issues[]`?** Separation of concerns:
- `issues[]` is for *new observations* the reviewer makes
- `finding_verdicts[]` is for *adjudicating existing mechanical findings*
- The import pipeline processes them differently (verdict -> lookup existing issue vs. issue -> create new)
- This also means existing reviewers that don't produce `finding_verdicts` continue working with zero changes

**Why reuse `concern_dismissals` for persistence?** The mechanism already:
- Uses stable fingerprints
- Stores source_issue_ids for lifecycle tracking
- Has `cleanup_stale_dismissals()` for garbage collection
- Is checked by `generate_concerns()` before surfacing concerns
- Survives rescans

The only extension needed is that `generate_concerns()` (and the prompt builder) should also check dismissals keyed on raw finding IDs, not just aggregated concern fingerprints. This is a small change: when building concern signals for the prompt, filter out findings whose IDs appear as `source_issue_ids` in any dismissal entry.

**Why not `resolve --status wontfix`?** That mechanism sets `status: "wontfix"` on the issue itself in state, which means it stays in scoring and is visible in the issue inventory. A finding dismissal is more like "this is intentional / not a real problem" -- it should prevent the finding from being surfaced as a concern signal in future reviews, but the mechanical finding should remain in state for scoring purposes. The concern_dismissals mechanism handles exactly this distinction.

### Prompt Template Design

The new prompt section would look approximately like:

```
JUDGMENT FINDINGS — explore and adjudicate:
The mechanical scan found findings from judgment-required detectors that need human review.
These detectors identify patterns that MAY be problems but require understanding of intent.

Available findings:
  - smells: 12 findings
  - structural: 4 findings  
  - naming: 8 findings

To explore these findings, run:
  desloppify show smells --output /tmp/smells.json --no-budget
  desloppify show structural --output /tmp/structural.json --no-budget
  desloppify show <detector> --output /tmp/<detector>.json --no-budget

For each finding you investigate, read the actual source code and record a verdict in
your output's finding_verdicts[] array:
  - "confirm": This is a real problem → promoted to a concern issue
  - "dismiss": This is intentional/acceptable → suppressed from future reviews  
  - "skip": Not enough information / defer to next review pass

You do not need to adjudicate every finding. Focus on findings in your batch's seed files
and findings that relate to your assigned dimension.
```

### Sequencing and Dependencies

1. Steps 3-4 (contracts + import processing) can be built and tested first with unit tests
2. Steps 1-2 (prompt changes) depend on step 6 (batch data) conceptually but can be developed in parallel
3. Step 5 (orchestrator wiring) depends on steps 3-4
4. End-to-end integration testing needs all steps

### Potential Challenges

1. **Finding ID stability**: Issue IDs like `structural::src/big_file.py` are stable across rescans as long as the file and detector are the same. The ID format is `<detector>::<file>::<detail_suffix>`. Dismissed findings that get a new ID after rescan (e.g., file renamed) will not be suppressed -- this is acceptable behavior since the code actually changed.

2. **Prompt length**: Adding finding exploration instructions adds to an already substantial prompt. Mitigate by only including this section for batches that include design_coherence or other finding-heavy dimensions, and keeping the instruction block brief.

3. **Reviewer compliance**: The reviewer may not produce `finding_verdicts` at all if it ignores that part of the schema. This is fine -- the array is optional and empty by default. The existing concern signal flow continues working.

4. **Dismissal fingerprint collisions**: Using `_fingerprint("finding_dismiss", finding_id, ())` should produce unique fingerprints since finding IDs themselves are unique. No collision risk with concern-level fingerprints since those use different type strings.

- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/app/commands/review/batch/prompt_template.py` - Extend output schema with `finding_verdicts[]`, add to `render_batch_prompt()`
- `/Users/user_c042661f/Documents/desloppify/desloppify/intelligence/review/importing/holistic_issue_flow.py` - Add `process_finding_verdicts()` for confirm/dismiss/skip logic
